## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008
F4) are bounded-composition restrictions the pricing model forced. Decide
the bound model (§6), keep the language, and re-derive its restrictions.

### 4.2 The first slice is at the scale where Snapback 2 looked elegant

§10 proposes "a private conversation with paginated messages and an unread
count," "two principals and two clients." Snapback 2 passed that slice in
its first week. It broke at the hub: 1,000 memberships refused the digest
fold, made global search cost a thousand requests, and refused the
envelopes builders first wrote (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). Every one of those
was discovered by a cold builder on a published package weeks after the
architecture was fixed. The first slice must include the hub principal from
day one (§11).

### 4.3 It investigates the client data model; the field has answered

§6 says "test a canonical local store with deliberate derived indexes,
durable pending operations, and explicit coverage and authorization
knowledge." That sentence describes a replica; the field has shipped it
under several names (buckets, mutators and a change stream, per-device
queues), Snapback 2's own LLP 1012 §6 reaches it by the hard road ("the
coverage rule that makes everything on the phone exact" is a fully drained
partition), and LLP 1019's `deliver` already computes the audience the
engine must maintain. Decide it (§7) and spend the investigation budget on
the two things the field has *not* settled for this design: partition
inversion of Quarry rules, and horizons at hub scale.

### 4.4 "Builder, human or agent" is two audiences

§3's first row conflates them. Agents need structured refusals with a
pasted rewrite, a one-screen grammar, and a served guide keyed by symbol.
Humans need a language server, a formatter, a dev overlay that shows the
live data and the queries watching it, and error text that reads as a
sentence. Snapback 2 measured only the agent (correction loops,
time-to-green) and shipped no editor support. "Beautiful" for the human
builder is mostly tooling, and it is cheap if it is in slice one and
expensive if it is retrofitted onto a parser that was never an LSP.

### 4.5 It inherits Snapback 2's process shape

# LLP 3000.011.003.000: Offline-ready application starters

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 web and Expo starters, client examples, acquisition measurement
**Author:** Codex / GPT-6 (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Revised:** 2026-10-12 (§7.6: wave 7 — the README and init summary say what works offline from the schema's synced tables; init names `--port N` as the one flag for both ports; the first read spreads `otherwiseAll` first); 2026-10-11 (§7.5: wave 6 — the README follows the screen and names `dev --port`, `app.json` ids named after the directory, `usePersonaApp` in the persona picker, `...otherwiseAll` in the first read, a backend-first quickstart); 2026-10-11 (§7: small default starters; the chat reference becomes `--template chat`; §7.1: a starter beside a project's own schema; §7.2: init first, schema second — `check` names starter leftovers, `init --adapt`; §7.3: the Expo starter's audit findings; §7.4: wave 4 — adapt keeps `fixtures/`, `snapback/generated/` is never a conflict, a neutral `Home` screen, `test` skips the unmodified starter rows, the advisories before the install)
**Related:** [Problem](./3000.011.003-offline-application-responsibilities.research.md), [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md), [LLP 3000.008.003](./3000.008.003-whole-application-performance.rfc.md)

## 1. Recommendation

Reuse the existing Vite chat reference as one supported production web output,
exposed through the existing init flow. Default init remains schema-only.
Demonstrate cached-route-first navigation, an offline-loadable shell and visible
pending writes that have no predicted row. Use existing client APIs. Add a complete application journey to
the existing starter/witness tests, rather than introduce another state API.

The current Expo starter already shows pending and stale reads. Do not claim
to add those APIs or expand into a multi-bundler framework. Native execution
is a separate actual-application witness in the evidence proposal.

This is an application composition change. Replica recovery and acquisition
semantics remain in their existing implementation and owning LLP.

## 2. Route and write behavior

For a route to a known conversation, read the held membership/conversation
first. Open a locally readable room without first issuing a canonical-DM
creation mutation. When the local answer is incomplete or unavailable, show
the existing read state and allow the online lookup if connected. A horizon
miss is not proof that the room does not exist. Creating a new DM remains an
explicit operation with its current online/eligibility requirements.

Keep `complete` and `fresh` visible through the example's rendering logic.
Do not equate stale with unusable, loading with empty, or mutation settlement
with a freshly observed replica. A navigation that specifically needs the
new authoritative projection can await that observation, but ordinary held
navigation must not become globally dependent on `sync()`.

Bind saved route and intent metadata to both backend identity and principal.
Persist the minimal ID metadata before dispatch when crash recovery requires
it, then observe the SDK receipt; never add a competing outbox or replay loop.
An unknown/evicted receipt remains unknown. Account B cannot see or submit A’s
intent, including through delayed callbacks. Offline sign-out must remain
possible and does not claim A’s pending writes settled or delete its replica.
Test account switch with pending nonpredicting writes and delayed work.
Completed sign-out means local credentials have been durably removed. A stalled
logout request or refresh response cannot gate local invalidation; a late
refresh or store write cannot restore the credential after completion. A
storage failure remains incomplete and retryable. Ordinary client close keeps
its existing session-preservation behavior; remote revocation is best effort.
A rotation already committed by the server must be allowed to return its
successor for best-effort logout, without adopting or persisting that result.

Use the existing pending-write hook and stable intent id when a mutation has
no predicted row. Reopen observes that same write; it does not mint a new id.
Predicted rows keep their existing `pending` indication. A failed write stays
visible and actionable instead of disappearing with a cleared composer.

## 3. Shell and platform behavior

For the supported production web starter, provide a build-known allowlist app-shell cache
for the build's immutable assets and navigation entry. Cache only immutable build assets and a generic nonpersonalized entry; do not
cache arbitrary same-origin responses or navigation HTML. Keep authenticated API
responses, tokens, uploaded media and user data out of that cache; the SDK
retains the data/media it owns. Scope the worker to the application and use a
versioned shell lifecycle so one deployment does not delete a still-running
tab's assets mid-session. Development hot reload should not use a stale
production cache.

If the starter's bundler already provides shell caching, configure that path
rather than install a competing worker. Make the supported output shape
explicit; do not promise arbitrary bundlers automatically acquire offline
reload. Test an actual new page realm with network unavailable.

Expo uses the installed bundle and published native device module. Expo Go's
online fallback remains explicit. No Hermes export is relabeled a durable
native execution result; the evidence proposal owns that execution.

## 4. Residency is a measured application choice

Connect the starter's instructions to the existing
`test --residency --fixture ... --as ...` command and actual client witnesses.
Show which tables supply the screen, which are fully acquired, and which use
a horizon. Reuse current diagnostics rather than add a new residency report
format. Do not infer real client memory from fixture row counts alone.

The paired residency experiment is deferred until two configurations prove
equivalent offline answers. It is not a required implementation deliverable.
For a future Clique measurement, retain two explicit configurations for a paired
experiment only when both meet the same required offline product scope: the
builder's broad public replication and a candidate using justified horizons
or server-only nonessential aggregates. Record transferred rows/bytes, first
useful content, completed durable acquisition, peak memory and offline reopen
on the real web and native clients. Report any reduction in offline history
or prediction separately; it cannot be claimed as an equivalent speedup.

## 5. Acceptance

Extend existing starter and witness scenarios to cover:

- Prime a room, terminate the app/page, stop the backend, and open its route
  with no attempted create mutation before rendering held history.
- Open an unknown room offline and preserve uncertainty instead of creating
  a duplicate or reporting authoritative absence.
- Persist two pending writes across process/page restart, then reconnect and
  verify ordered settlement with the original ids and no duplicate rows.
- Render a pending nonpredicting write, a failed write and a stale retained
  read through the existing card without an application shadow queue.
- Upgrade the web shell with an older tab open; sign out/switch accounts and
  verify the shell cache contains no authenticated response or media bytes.

Success is a fresh builder retaining those properties while replacing the
starter's business screens. Keep generated/example code volume, route
corrections and measured acquisition beside the result. No new push gate or
global cache service is proposed. If a generic shell solution expands into a
framework, limit the first increment to the one published starter and record
the unsupported build shapes.

# Wire: HTTP for curl, scripts and non-JS clients

Under the JS client is plain JSON over HTTP, which curl, Python, Swift or any HTTP client can speak. Each call below is a POST with a JSON body unless it says GET.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s -X POST $U/q/mine -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{}}'
curl -s -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"body":"hi"}}'
```

`snapback4 dev` serves on that address (choose another with `--port N`, or
`--port 0` for a free one; the first stdout line is the URL). A taken port
refuses the start at once with `E_ADDRINUSE`, naming the holder's pid. The JS client (`snapback4/client`) stays the
paved surface for apps: it adds sync, offline writes and live queries.

## Identity

In development, `x-snapback-persona: alice` acts as `dev:alice`: same machine
only, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, and only for a name `use personas` declares; any other
name is 401 E_AUTH naming the declaration (a schema without `use personas`
admits any name). `snapback4 serve` refuses personas. Everywhere else send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, from
`/auth/guest` (`{}`), `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login` (`{"email","password"}`),
each answering `{"session":{principal,kind,token,expiresAt}}`; a worker's token
comes from `snapback4 token --service <name>`. Send one credential, not both.

## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}`; never treat it as an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

## Operations

- `/q/<op>` with `{"args":{..}}` answers `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer}`
  or `{denied}`. `data` is what the query returns, as is: a list, a row, a shape,
  or a scalar (a keyed view read `totals[owner]` answers the number itself).
  Page by passing `next` back as the query's cursor argument.
- `/m/<op>` with `{"id"?, "args":{..}}` answers `{state:"sent", id, seq, result}` or
  `{state:"failed", id, why, current?}`, both HTTP 200 (see Refusals for the
  status codes). `result` is what the mutation returns (an inserted row's id is
  `result.id`); the top-level `id` is the write id you sent, omitted without one. With an `id` (a unique string the caller mints once per
  intended write), a retry of the same call applies at most once and answers
  `replayed: true`; the same `id` with another operation or other arguments is
  `{state:"failed", why:{code:"E_ID_REUSED"}}`. Without an `id`, every POST is a
  new write.
  A replay omits `result` and `current`: the durable receipt retains execution
  identity, not historical values or read authority. Read current data with a
  query instead of treating an absent replay result as a new-write result.
- Values: `time` is Unix milliseconds; `bytes` is lowercase hex; a reference is
  its row's id string. Numbers: see below.
- Uploads: `POST /assets` with the file's bytes as the raw body (not JSON, not multipart; the
  server sniffs JPEG, PNG, WebP or MP4 and ignores `Content-Type`), as the uploader, answers
  `{asset: {id, type, bytes, width, height, duration}}`, or `{denied}`: `E_ASSET_TYPE` (other
  bytes), `E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE`, 400 for an empty body, 401 `E_AUTH`, 413 past 64 MiB. Pass the
  asset to a mutation's `image`/`video`/`file` argument as `{"id": "<asset id>"}`; `GET /assets/<id>`
  returns the bytes to a viewer the row's read rule admits, else 404 `E_ASSET_UNKNOWN`.
  `curl -X POST $U/assets -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' --data-binary @receipt.png`
- A job is not a client call (E_OP): a schedule fires it, and in dev
  `/dev/jobs/<job>` or `snapback4 run <job>` runs one.
- GET `/changes?since=<seq>&wait=25` long-polls. It answers `{seq, tables,
  generation, store_id}` when a commit lands or after `wait` seconds: re-query
  what reads those tables, then poll again from the new `seq`. `/sync` is the
  device replication protocol; use the client rather than reimplementing it.
- A held wait is bounded. A full host answers 503 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 2);
  one principal past its share of held polls (one per tab or device, default
  16) gets 429 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 5). Both are retryable and admitted
  nothing: wait for Retry-After, poll `/changes?since=<seq>` without `wait`
  meanwhile, then hold again. The JS client does this for you.
- A host that runs the device itself (the native module or the wasm device
  without `snapback4/client`) must route each device `query` reply: some say
  "ask the server" (`unknown` without `retained`) and carry a placeholder
  `data`. "The device reply" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §7 is that contract.

## Numbers, money and decimals

Every number on the wire is a JSON integer within ±(2^53 − 1); there are no
floats. `money CUR` (scale 2 unless given) and `decimal S` hold an integer
scaled by 10^S and travel as that integer, in both directions:

```quarry
table expenses:
  owner: principal
  amount: money USD                 -- $12.50 travels as 1250
  rate: decimal 4                   -- 0.0725 travels as 725
  place: json <=400 ?               -- numbers inside are integers too
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
mutation addExpense(amount: money USD, rate: decimal 4, place: json <=400 ?):
  require amount > 0 else BAD_AMOUNT
  return insert expenses { owner: viewer, amount, rate, place }
query spent():
  return sum(expenses where .owner = viewer, .amount)
```

```sh
curl -s -X POST $U/m/addExpense -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"args":{"amount":1250,"rate":725,"place":{"latE6":37774900,"lngE6":-122419400}}}'
```

`spent` answers `{"data":1250,..}`: cents, not dollars. `12.5` and `1000.0`
refuse HTTP 400 E_INPUT ("argument amount: an integer"), `"12.50"` too ("expected
an integer amount in minor units"), and so does a fraction anywhere inside a
`json` value ("a number must be an integer in the signed 53-bit range"). Scale with rounding before sending:
`Math.round(dollars * 100)`, since `0.29 * 100` is `28.999999999999996`; in
Python send `round(x * 100)`, because `json.dumps(10.0)` writes `10.0`.
Format on display, `(cents / 100).toFixed(2)`. Keep coordinates and
measurements as scaled integers (microdegrees, milligrams) or as text.

## Development doors

Dev only, same machine; `serve` refuses each one.

- `/seed` with a JSON array `[{"table":"t","row":{"id":"..",..}}, ..]` writes
  every row as the system in one commit, rules bypassed (types, constraints,
  caps and sync still apply), and answers `{seeded: N}`; anything else is 400
  E_INPUT, and a row the engine refuses answers 200 `{denied}` with nothing
  written. Rows are written like inserts: omitted columns take their
  `default` (`now` is the dev clock), sequence columns are assigned and must be
  omitted (E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED), and the `$`-prefixed fields `snapback4 data`
  prints are recorded by the engine itself: drop them before seeding. It is an upsert by `id`: every row
  names its own (any unique string; `idFrom(label)` gives a stable one), a
  reseed updates the mutable columns of an existing row, and a different value
  for an `immutable` column refuses E_CONSTRAINT ("… is immutable; /seed is an
  upsert by id …"): keep the original value, use a new id, `POST /dev/reset`
  first, or restart dev with `--memory`. A principal field holds `"dev:alice"`,
  not `"alice"`.
- `/dev/reset` empties the store for the next test, in one transaction: every
  application row, view and maintained column, the sync log, the effect, job
  and action queues, uploaded assets, and accounts with their sessions
  (signed-up users, guests). The adopted schema, personas and service seats
  (`snapback4 token`, the dev worker's) stay. The store takes a new identity:
  a local client re-acquires the new store by itself, an online client halts
  with `E_STORE_REPLACED` (`createApp` reopens it). The head keeps counting.
  The body is empty, a `/seed` array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}`
  (either key optional): seed rows are written as `/seed` writes them, in the
  same transaction (a refused row resets nothing), and `"clock": "reset"`
  drops `--clock-offset` and every advance (by default the dev clock stays).
  It answers `{reset: true, seeded: N, generation, storeId, previousStoreId,
  head, now, clockOffsetMs, ms}` in a few milliseconds on a small store;
  `snapback4 reset [--port N] [--seed fixtures] [--clock reset]` posts it.
- `/dev/clock/advance` with `{"duration":"+2m"}` moves the dev clock, sweeps
  expiry, fires due schedules and runs queued jobs (`snapback4 clock advance 2m`).
- `/dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}` runs one job now and answers its run.
- GET `/dev/status` (no credential) is the readiness probe: it names the
  server's `pid`, `project`, whether the store is `temporary`, the served
  generation, the dev clock and the declared personas.
- `snapback4 run <op> --as bob` beside a running dev forwards to these routes.

## Refusals

| HTTP | Meaning / body |
|---|---|
| 200 | Operation success, or decoded application refusal (`denied` / `state:"failed", why`); inspect the body |
| 202 | An admitted asynchronous action is still running |
| 400 | Malformed JSON/wire arguments or wrong operation door, `denied` |
| 401 | Missing/dead credential, production persona, failed login or disabled identity method, `denied` E_AUTH |
| 403 | Development door on serve, read-only token attempting a write, or supervised-worker refresh, `denied` |
| 404 | Unknown route/operation, or hidden/missing asset, `denied` |
| 413 | Request exceeds the body ceiling, `denied` |
| 429 | Identity rate limit or per-principal wait capacity; retry as directed |
| 500 | Internal store/response-metadata failure, `denied` |
| 503 | Retryable refusal, store/infrastructure failure or host capacity; retry as directed |

Authentication refuses before an operation executes. Identity helpers in the
JS client preserve the `denied` body on 401/403; do not replace it with a proxy
error page. Rule/constraint refusals after operation admission remain HTTP 200.
Identity scope/value validation can also return 200 denied/E_INPUT; inspect the body.
After a streaming heartbeat starts, HTTP stays 200; inspect the final JSON for
`denied` if the session expires while waiting.

Two layers answer differently. The HTTP layer decodes the request against the
operation's signature before anything runs: a body that is not JSON, an
argument of the wrong JSON type or shape (a fraction, a string for an `int`,
a number for `text`), or an argument the operation does not take is **400**
`{denied:{code:"E_INPUT"}}`. No or a dead credential (or an undeclared
persona) is **401** E_AUTH; an unknown operation or route is **404** E_OP, and
a declared one at the wrong door (a job or query at `/m/`, a mutation at
`/q/`) is **400** E_OP naming the right one; a body past its ceiling (1 MiB of JSON, 16 MiB for `/seed` and `/dev/reset`) is **413**. Once
decoded, the operation runs, and everything it refuses is **200**: a query
answers `{denied}`, a mutation `{state:"failed", why}`. That includes value
checks made while it runs — a missing required argument, text outside its
bounds, `''` for a `text 1..500` argument too (`why.code` E_INPUT) — rules (E_RULE), constraints
(E_CONSTRAINT), a delete that rows still reference (E_REFERENCED, naming the
referencing column), `NOT_FOUND`, the program's own `require … else CODE` (with
`why.site` and `why.rule`), and a reused write id (E_ID_REUSED). A retryable
refusal is 503 instead. So branch on the body, not only the status; for an
`add(body: text <=200)` mutation:

```sh
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":12.5}}'
# {"denied":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"argument body: expected text …"}} 400
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":"'"$(printf 'x%.0s' {1..300})"'"}}'
# {"state":"failed","why":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"E_INPUT: body is longer than 200 characters"}} 200
```

A refusal is `{code, family, message, site?, rule?, rewrite?, guide?}`, and
`snapback4 why <code>` explains one. `E_WRITE_ID_REUSE` is the JS client's own
local refusal for the same mistake, before anything is sent; the wire's code is
`E_ID_REUSED`.

## Uploads and downloads

`POST /assets` takes the raw bytes as the body and answers `{"asset":{id,type,bytes,…}}`
or `{denied}`; a mutation then places `{"id": …}` in its `image`, `video` or `file`
argument. An image or video is sniffed from its bytes. A document for a `file`
column adds `?as=file&name=<percent-encoded filename>` and declares its type,
which the server checks against the bytes (`guide assets`, "Files"):

```sh
curl -s -X POST "$U/assets?as=file&name=March%20receipt.pdf" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/pdf' --data-binary @receipt.pdf
# {"asset":{"id":"…","type":"application/pdf","bytes":48213,"name":"March receipt.pdf","width":null,"height":null,"duration":null}}
curl -s -OJ "$U/assets/<id>" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice'   # Content-Disposition: attachment names the file
```

`GET /assets/<id>` is 404 E_ASSET_UNKNOWN when hidden or gone; a file always
arrives as an attachment, and `Range` works on ordinary assets.

The full reference, including the rest of `/auth`, worker and action
doors, and clock headers, is "The wire" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §5, which
ships in the package beside README.md.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
//! Starter leftovers on the init-first path: `init --web` (or `--expo`) wrote
//! the todo starter, then the project replaced `snapback/schema.q`. The
//! starter's own files that still match the bytes init wrote, but no longer
//! fit the schema, are leftovers: `check` names them and `init --adapt`
//! removes or rewrites them. A file the project changed is never touched.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.2 — leftovers are judged by the starter's original bytes, never by name alone.
use super::starter::{self, Body, Platform};
use super::{OwnSchema, own_schema};
use std::path::Path;

/// One unmodified starter file that no longer fits the project's schema.
pub(crate) struct Leftover {
    pub path: &'static str,
    pub why: String,
    /// The text `init --adapt` writes; `None` removes the file.
    pub rewrite: Option<String>,
}

/// Starter operations its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
const STARTER_OPERATIONS: [&str; 4] = ["list", "add", "toggle", "remove"];

/// Whether a schema still has the starter's `todos` table and the operations
/// its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
pub(super) fn fits_todos(own: &OwnSchema) -> bool {
    own.tables.iter().any(|table| table == "todos")
        && STARTER_OPERATIONS.iter().all(|op| own.operations.iter().any(|name| name == op))
}

/// What the schema in `dir` leaves of the starter, and the advice that goes
/// with it (a sign-in the project must write by hand). No schema: nothing.
pub(crate) fn leftovers(dir: &Path) -> (Vec<Leftover>, Vec<String>) {
    let Ok(Some(own)) = own_schema(dir) else { return (Vec::new(), Vec::new()) };
    let mut found = Vec::new();
    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    let todos = fits_todos(&own);
    if !todos {
        for (path, text) in starter::FIXTURES {
            if read(dir, path).is_some_and(|current| current == super::without_design_references(text)) {
                let what = if path.ends_with("todos.jsonl") { "rows for the todo starter's `todos` table" } else { "cases for the todo starter's list/add/toggle/remove" };
                found.push(Leftover { path, why: format!("the unmodified {what}; your snapback/*.q has no todos table with those operations, so `snapback4 test` would refuse it"), rewrite: None });
            }
        }
    }
    for platform in [Platform::Web, Platform::Expo] {
        screens(dir, platform, &own, todos, &mut found, &mut advice);
    }
    readme(dir, &own, todos, &mut found);
    app_json(dir, &mut found);
    (found, advice)
}

/// The starter README, while it is still one init wrote (for either screen,
/// at the ports it names), when it describes another screen than the schema
/// now gets: the Todos README beside a project's schema, or the reverse.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (042): adapt rewrote the screen but left a README about todos and guest sign-in.
fn readme(dir: &Path, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    let platform = if dir.join("src/App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Web } else if dir.join("App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Expo } else { return };
    let title = super::title(dir);
    let personas = own.identity.is_none() && !own.personas.is_empty();
    let wanted_body = if todos { Body::Todos } else { Body::Home(None) };
    for path in ["README.md", "README.snapback.md"] {
        let Some(text) = read(dir, path) else { continue };
        let port = number_after(&text, "127.0.0.1:");
        let web_port = number_after(&text, "localhost:");
        let ports = [(port, web_port), (None, web_port), (port, None), (None, None)];
        // The offline paragraph names the schema's synced tables when init
        // wrote it; any of them (or 0.4.22's unconditional claim) still matches.
        let masked = starter::without_offline(&text);
        let matched = ports.into_iter().find(|&(port, web_port)| {
            [(Body::Todos, false), (Body::Home(None), false), (Body::Home(None), true)].iter()
                .any(|(body, personas)| masked == starter::without_offline(&super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::readme(platform, &title, body, *personas, &starter::Offline::Unknown)), port, web_port)))
        });
        let Some((port, web_port)) = matched else { continue };
        let wanted = super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::readme(platform, &title, &wanted_body, personas, &own.offline)), port, web_port);
        if wanted == text { continue; }
        let about = if todos { "the todo starter's operations, which your snapback/*.q declares again" } else { "your snapback/*.q and the Home screen, named after this directory" };
        let only_offline = !text.starts_with("# Todos") && starter::without_offline(&wanted) == masked;
        // A schema that does not compile right now says nothing about what it syncs.
        if only_offline && own.offline == starter::Offline::Unknown { continue; }
        let why = if only_offline {
            "the unmodified starter README says what works offline for another schema; adapt rewrites that paragraph from the tables your snapback/*.q syncs".to_owned()
        } else {
            format!("the unmodified starter README describes {}; adapt rewrites it to describe {about}", if text.starts_with("# Todos") { "the todo starter (its todos table and guest sign-in)" } else { "the Home screen for another schema" })
        };
        found.push(Leftover { path, why, rewrite: Some(wanted) });
    }
}

/// An Expo `app.json` that still carries the todo starter's slug, scheme and
/// bundle ids (written by 0.4.21 and earlier): adapt names them after the
/// directory and changes nothing else in the file.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (067): adapted Expo apps kept dev.snapback4.todos.
fn app_json(dir: &Path, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    let Some(text) = read(dir, "app.json") else { return };
    let Ok(config) = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&text) else { return };
    let expo = &config["expo"];
    let [(_, slug), (_, scheme), (_, bundle)] = starter::TODO_IDS;
    if expo["slug"] != slug || expo["scheme"] != scheme || expo["ios"]["bundleIdentifier"] != bundle || expo["android"]["package"] != bundle { return; }
    let (new_slug, new_scheme, new_bundle) = starter::app_ids(&super::title(dir));
    let quoted = |value: &str| format!("\"{value}\"");
    let rewrite = text.replace(&quoted(bundle), &quoted(&new_bundle)).replace(&quoted(slug), &quoted(&new_slug)).replace(&quoted(scheme), &quoted(&new_scheme));
    found.push(Leftover { path: "app.json", why: format!("the todo starter's ids (slug {slug}, scheme {scheme}, bundle id {bundle}); adapt names them after this directory ({new_slug}, {new_scheme}, {new_bundle})"), rewrite: Some(rewrite) });
}

fn number_after(text: &str, prefix: &str) -> Option<u16> {
    let at = text.find(prefix)? + prefix.len();
    let digits: String = text[at..].chars().take_while(char::is_ascii_digit).collect();
    digits.parse().ok()
}

/// The sign-in a schema admits, as the starter screen it needs: the guest
/// screen (`None`), the persona picker (`Some(personas)`), or no starter
/// screen fits (`Err(advice)`).
fn wanted(own: &OwnSchema) -> Result<Option<Vec<String>>, &'static str> {
    match own.identity {
        Some((_, true)) => Ok(None),
        Some((_, false)) => Ok(None),
        None if !own.personas.is_empty() => Ok(Some(own.personas.clone())),
        None => Err("declares neither `use identity` nor `use personas`, so nobody can act: add `use identity` for the guest sign-in or `use personas alice, bob` for development, then rerun `snapback4 init --adapt`"),
    }
}

fn screens(dir: &Path, platform: Platform, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>, advice: &mut Vec<String>) {
    let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
    let (Some(app), Some(client)) = (read(dir, app_path), read(dir, client_path)) else { return };
    // Which starter screen is on disk, if both files are still as init wrote
    // them: its sign-in (guest, or a persona picker) and its body.
    let journal_shape = starter::Offline::Tables { synced: vec![], total: 0 };
    let client_shape = normalized(&client);
    let matches_client = |reference: &str| client_shape == normalized(reference)
        || client_shape == normalized(&starter::web_client(reference.to_owned(), &journal_shape));
    let current = [Body::Todos, Body::Home(first_read(&app))].into_iter().find_map(|body| {
        let (guest_app, guest_client) = starter::screens(platform, None, &body);
        let (persona_app, persona_client) = starter::screens(platform, Some(&[]), &body);
        let (password_app, password_client) = starter::identity_screens(platform, None, &body, true);
        if app == guest_app && matches_client(&guest_client) { Some((None, body)) }
        else if app == password_app && matches_client(&password_client) { Some((None, body)) }
        else if app == persona_app && matches_client(&persona_client) { Some((Some(personas_of(&client)), body)) }
        else { None }
    });
    let Some((sign_in, body)) = current else { return };
    // A Home keeps its read while the schema still declares that query
    // argument-free; a Home without one stays as written (it fits any schema).
    let wanted_body = match &body {
        _ if todos => Body::Todos,
        Body::Home(None) => Body::Home(None),
        Body::Home(Some(op)) if own.reads.contains(op) => body.clone(),
        _ => Body::Home(own.reads.first().cloned()),
    };
    // A sign-in no starter screen fits keeps the one on disk, with advice.
    let wanted_sign_in = match wanted(own) {
        Ok(wanted) => wanted,
        Err(why) => {
            advice.push(format!("{app_path} is the starter's {}, but your snapback/*.q {why}", if sign_in.is_none() { "guest sign-in" } else { "persona picker" }));
            sign_in.clone()
        }
    };
    let (app_text, mut client_text) = starter::identity_screens(platform, wanted_sign_in.as_deref(), &wanted_body, own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests));
    client_text = starter::web_client(client_text, &own.offline);
    let client_changed = client_shape != normalized(&client_text);
    if app == app_text && wanted_body == body && !client_changed { return; }
    let mut reasons = Vec::new();
    let mut client_leftover = None;
    if wanted_sign_in != sign_in {
        let (from, to) = match (&sign_in, &wanted_sign_in) {
            (None, Some(personas)) => ("the guest sign-in".to_owned(), format!("a persona picker ({})", personas.join(", "))),
            (Some(_), None) => ("a persona picker".to_owned(), "the guest sign-in".to_owned()),
            (Some(old), Some(new)) => (format!("a picker for {}", old.join(", ")), format!("one for {}", new.join(", "))),
            (None, None) => unreachable!(),
        };
        let because = if wanted_sign_in.is_some() { "your snapback/*.q declares `use personas` and no `use identity`" } else { "your snapback/*.q declares `use identity`" };
        reasons.push(format!("the unmodified starter screen shows {from}, but {because}; adapt writes {to}"));
        // Keep the backend port init wrote.
        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
        client_leftover = Some(Leftover { path: client_path, why: format!("the unmodified starter client for {from}; adapt writes {to}"), rewrite: Some(client_text.clone()) });
    }
    if wanted_sign_in == sign_in && client_changed {
        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
        let why = "the unmodified web client needs the device or principal journal selected by your schema's synced tables".to_owned();
        reasons.push(why.clone());
        client_leftover = Some(Leftover { path: client_path, why, rewrite: Some(client_text.clone()) });
    }
    if wanted_body != body {
        reasons.push(match (&wanted_body, &body) {
            (Body::Home(_), Body::Home(Some(op))) => format!("its Home screen reads api.{op}, which your snapback/*.q no longer declares as a query without required arguments; adapt writes the Home screen again{}", match &wanted_body { Body::Home(Some(next)) => format!(", reading api.{next}"), _ => String::new() }),
            (Body::Home(_), _) => "its Todos screen calls the starter's list/add/toggle/remove, which your snapback/*.q does not declare, so it would not typecheck; adapt writes a Home screen that compiles against any schema (the signed-in viewer, your operations from snapback/generated/api.ts, and your first query without required arguments rendered with matchRead) for you to replace".to_owned(),
            (Body::Todos, _) => "your snapback/*.q declares the todo starter's operations again; adapt writes the Todos screen back".to_owned(),
        });
    }
    let why = reasons.join("; ");
    let why = if why.starts_with("the unmodified") { why } else { format!("the unmodified starter screen: {why}") };
    found.push(Leftover { path: app_path, why, rewrite: Some(app_text) });
    found.extend(client_leftover);
}

/// The `api` path a starter Home's first read calls, if it has one.
fn first_read(app: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let after = app.split_once("function FirstRead()")?.1;
    let op = after.split_once("useQuery(api.")?.1.split_once(',')?.0;
    Some(op.to_owned())
}

fn read(dir: &Path, path: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let path = dir.join(path);
    path.symlink_metadata().ok().filter(|metadata| metadata.is_file())?;
    std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok()
}

/// A starter client without what init or the schema varies: the backend port
/// and the persona list.
fn normalized(client: &str) -> String {
    let lines = client.split_inclusive('\n').map(|line| {
        if line.starts_with(PERSONAS) { format!("{PERSONAS}[];\n") } else { line.to_owned() }
    }).collect::<String>();
    match port_of(&lines) { Some(port) => lines.replace(&format!("127.0.0.1:{port}"), "127.0.0.1:4400"), None => lines }
}

const PERSONAS: &str = "export const personas: readonly string[] = ";

fn personas_of(client: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    client.lines().find_map(|line| line.strip_prefix(PERSONAS))
        .and_then(|list| serde_json::from_str(list.trim_end().trim_end_matches(';')).ok()).unwrap_or_default()
}

fn port_of(client: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    let at = client.find("127.0.0.1:")? + "127.0.0.1:".len();
    let digits = client[at..].find(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(client.len() - at);
    (digits > 0).then(|| &client[at..at + digits])
}

/// One `check` line, or none: the leftovers and the command that fixes them.
pub(crate) fn report(dir: &Path) -> Option<String> {
    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return None; }
    let mut lines = Vec::new();
    for leftover in &found { lines.push(format!("  {}: {}", leftover.path, leftover.why)); }
    for line in &advice { lines.push(format!("  {line}")); }
    if !found.is_empty() {
        lines.push("  fix: snapback4 init --adapt (removes or rewrites only these files, which still match the starter; your own files are never touched)".into());
    }
    Some(format!("starter: {} left from `snapback4 init` that {} not fit your schema\n{}",
        if found.is_empty() { "a screen".to_owned() } else { format!("{} file{}", found.len(), if found.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }) },
        if found.len() == 1 || found.is_empty() { "does" } else { "do" }, lines.join("\n")))
}

/// The same, for `check --json`: `null` when nothing is left over.
pub(crate) fn report_json(dir: &Path) -> serde_json::Value {
    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return serde_json::Value::Null; }
    serde_json::json!({
        "files": found.iter().map(|leftover| serde_json::json!({ "path": leftover.path, "why": leftover.why, "adapt": if leftover.rewrite.is_some() { "rewrite" } else { "remove" } })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        "advice": advice,
        "fix": if found.is_empty() { serde_json::Value::Null } else { "snapback4 init --adapt".into() },
    })
}

/// `snapback4 init --adapt [dir]`.
pub(super) fn adapt(dir: &Path) -> i32 {
    match own_schema(dir) {
        Ok(Some(_)) => {}
        Ok(None) => { eprintln!("init --adapt: no snapback/*.q in {}; nothing to adapt to (run `snapback4 init --web` or `--expo` first)", dir.display()); return 1; }
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("init: {error}"); return 2; }
    }
    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
    let mut removed = Vec::new();
    let mut rewritten = Vec::new();
    for leftover in &found {
        let path = dir.join(leftover.path);
        let result = match &leftover.rewrite {
            Some(text) => super::replace_file(&path, text).map(|()| rewritten.push(leftover.path)),
            None => std::fs::remove_file(&path).map(|()| removed.push(leftover.path)),
        };
        if let Err(error) = result { eprintln!("init --adapt: {}: {error}", path.display()); return 2; }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — adapt removes the files it names, never a directory: the next step writes fixtures/<table>.jsonl.
    if found.is_empty() { println!("init --adapt: nothing to adapt — no unmodified starter file conflicts with your snapback/*.q"); }
    if !removed.is_empty() {
        let kept = if removed.iter().any(|path| path.starts_with("fixtures/")) { "; fixtures/ stays for your own rows" } else { "" };
        println!("init --adapt: removed {} (the todo starter's samples{kept}; write your own as fixtures/<table>.jsonl and fixtures/test.cases.jsonl, `snapback4 guide testing`)", removed.join(" and "));
    }
    if !rewritten.is_empty() {
        let why = found.iter().find(|leftover| leftover.rewrite.is_some()).map(|leftover| leftover.why.as_str()).unwrap_or_default();
        println!("init --adapt: rewrote {} — {why}", rewritten.join(" and "));
    }
    if found.iter().any(|leftover| leftover.rewrite.as_deref().is_some_and(|text| text.contains("function Home("))) {
        println!("init --adapt: next: `snapback4 check` writes snapback/generated/api.ts for the Home screen; replace Home with your own screens (`snapback4 guide client`)");
    }
    for line in advice { println!("init --adapt: {line}"); }
    0
}
