//! `test.cases.jsonl` beside the fixture tables: the application's own
//! operations, in order, as named viewers, each with the outcome it expects.
//! One line is `{"as": "alice", "op": "addCard", "args": {..}, "expect": "sent"}`;
//! `expect` is `sent` (a mutation's default), `complete`, `capped`, `ok` (any
//! answer), or a refusal code (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, a program's own `require`
//! code). A query's default is its `check` verdict: `complete`, or either
//! answer when `check` notes the query is never complete (N_NEVER_COMPLETE).
//! Optional `data` must equal the answer; `count` is the answer's length; `save`
//! names the answer so a later string argument `"$name"` or `"$name.field"`
//! reuses it. `as` is a declared persona, as on dev: an undeclared one is
//! refused E_AUTH.
use crate::project::persona_principal;
use snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace;
use snapback4_core::ir::ProgramKind;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, WriteState, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

pub const FILE: &str = "test.cases.jsonl";

/// Dev's cast check, for `test` and a local `run`: with `use personas`
/// declared, a bare name (or `dev:<name>`) outside the cast is refused as dev
/// refuses its header (E_AUTH); a principal id such as `user:…` passes as itself.
pub fn undeclared_persona(name: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Option<String> {
    if declared.is_empty() { return None; }
    let bare = match name.split_once(':') { None => name, Some(("dev", bare)) => bare, Some(_) => return None };
    (!declared.iter().any(|p| p == bare)).then(|| format!(
        "persona {bare} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast, and dev refuses {bare} with E_AUTH (401). Add {bare} to it, or use a declared persona (an outsider is a declared persona with no rows)",
        declared.join(", ")))
}

/// The persona a case without `as` runs as: alice when she is in the cast (or
/// there is none), else the first declared persona.
pub fn default_persona(declared: &[String]) -> &str {
    if declared.is_empty() || declared.iter().any(|p| p == "alice") { "alice" } else { &declared[0] }
}

/// One case as `test`'s query sweep reads it: which query, as whom, with
/// which arguments (`None` when they name a saved answer, which only the case
/// run has), expecting what.
pub struct Explicit { pub at: String, pub op: String, pub principal: String, pub args: Option<Json>, pub expect: Option<String> }

/// The cases file read leniently, before the sweep: a line that does not
/// parse is left to [`run`], which refuses it with its line number.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: the sweep runs a persona's own case arguments and labels a refusal its case expects.
pub fn explicit(fixture: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Vec<Explicit> {
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(fixture).join(FILE)) else { return Vec::new() };
    fn saved(value: &Json) -> bool {
        match value { Json::String(text) => text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1, Json::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), Json::Object(fields) => fields.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    }
    text.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(index, line)| {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { return None; }
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).ok()?;
        let op = case.get("op")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(declared));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        let args = Some(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}))).filter(|args| args.is_object() && !saved(args));
        Some(Explicit { at: format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1), op, principal, args, expect: case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str).map(str::to_owned) })
    }).collect()
}

/// Runs every case; prints one line each and returns (passed, failed).
/// `sources` are the compiled files, to quote a refusing clause's line.
pub fn run(engine: &mut Engine, bound: &Bound, fixture: &str, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let path = Path::new(fixture).join(FILE);
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
        Ok(text) => text,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((0, 0)),
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display())),
    };
    let mut saved = BTreeMap::<String, Json>::new();
    let (mut passed, mut failed) = (0, 0);
    let mut verdicts_shown = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let lines: Vec<(usize, &str)> = text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.trim_start().starts_with("//")).collect();
    // Every line is checked before any runs: a misspelled key must not pass
    // silently (wave 5: `"rows":0` passed against one row), and a typo on the
    // last line should not wait for every earlier case.
    let mut cases = Vec::with_capacity(lines.len());
    for (index, line) in &lines {
        let at = format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1);
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
        validate(&case).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        cases.push((at, case));
    }
    println!("test: cases from {FILE}");
    for (at, case) in cases {
        let Some(op) = case.get("op").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return Err(format!("{at}: each case names \"op\" (a query or mutation)")) };
        let Some(bound_program) = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op) else {
            let starter = super::init::starter_fixture(FILE).is_some_and(|starter| starter == text);
            let hint = if starter { format!("\n  hint: {FILE} is the todo starter's unmodified cases for list/add/toggle/remove; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it (write your own cases, `snapback4 guide testing`)") } else { String::new() };
            return Err(format!("{at}: unknown operation {op}{hint}"));
        };
        let program = bound_program.program.clone();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(&bound.personas));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        // A query's default expectation is check's verdict: a noted never-complete page passes capped.
        let never_complete = (program.kind == ProgramKind::Query).then(|| never_complete(bound, bound_program)).flatten();
        let explicit = case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str);
        let expect = explicit.unwrap_or(match program.kind { ProgramKind::Query if never_complete.is_some() => "ok", ProgramKind::Query => "complete", _ => "sent" });
        let label = case.get("label").and_then(Json::as_str).map(|l| format!(" — {l}")).unwrap_or_default();
        let raw_args = substitute(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({})), &saved).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
        if let Json::Object(fields) = &raw_args {
            for (name, value) in fields {
                let Some(kind) = program.args.get(name) else { return Err(format!("{at}: {op} takes no argument {name}")) };
                args.insert(name.clone(), wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: argument {name}: {why}"))?);
            }
        } else { return Err(format!("{at}: \"args\" is an object")); }
        let trace = RefusalTrace::default();
        let context = Context::new(principal, args).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
        // (outcome word, answer) or a refusal
        let mut unavailable = false;
        let got: Result<(&str, Json), Refusal> = if let Some(why) = undeclared_persona(persona, &bound.personas) {
            Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", why))
        } else { match program.kind {
            ProgramKind::Query => match engine.query(op, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => { unavailable = outcome.unavailable; Ok((if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" }, wire::to_json(&outcome.data))) },
                Err(refusal) => Err(refusal),
            },
            ProgramKind::Job => return Err(format!("{at}: {op} is a job; cases run queries and mutations")),
            _ => match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                    WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => Ok(("sent", wire::to_json(&result))),
                    WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => Err(why),
                },
                Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
            },
        } };
        let mut problems = Vec::new();
        let mut notes = Vec::new();
        let shown = match &got {
            Ok((word, answer)) => {
                let admitted = *word == expect || expect == "ok" || (expect == "complete" && *word == "sent");
                if !admitted { problems.push(if expect == "capped" && *word == "complete" {
                    "now complete — update the case: replace expect: \"capped\" with expect: \"complete\" (or omit expect); readable pinned-parent guards can now certify this page".to_owned()
                } else { format!("expected {expect}, got {word}") }); }
                if *word == "capped" && let Some(note) = &never_complete {
                    if admitted && explicit.is_none() && verdicts_shown.insert(op.to_owned()) { notes.push(format!("capped is this query's check verdict: {note}")); }
                    if !admitted { notes.push(format!("hint: {note}; a capped answer still holds every row you may read: add \"expect\":\"capped\" (or drop \"expect\"), or pin the page as the note's rewrite says (snapback4 check --verbose)")); }
                }
                if let Some(data) = case.get("data") && data != answer {
                    problems.push(format!("data differs: got {answer}"));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.12 — an unavailable answer is not a null value: say why it has none.
                    if unavailable { notes.push("null here is no answer (a client shows `loading`), not a null value: a ceiling cut it, or an `exists` over rows this viewer only partly reads found no row it may read, which cannot prove absence (snapback4 check --verbose)".into()); }
                }
                if let Some(count) = case.get("count").and_then(Json::as_u64) {
                    let length = answer.as_array().map(|a| a.len()).or_else(|| answer.get("items").and_then(Json::as_array).map(|a| a.len()));
                    if length != Some(count as usize) { problems.push(format!("expected {count} rows, got {}", length.map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| "a non-list".into()))); }
                }
                if let Some(name) = case.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) { saved.insert(name.to_owned(), answer.clone()); }
                (*word).to_owned()
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                let code = &refusal.code;
                if code != expect && !(expect == "denied" || expect == "refused" || expect == "failed") {
                    problems.push(format!("expected {expect}, got {code}: {}", refusal.message));
                    notes.extend(refusal_evidence(&trace, refusal, bound, sources, explicit));
                }
                code.clone()
            }
        };
        if problems.is_empty() {
            passed += 1;
            println!("      ok   {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}");
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            println!("      FAIL {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}: {}", problems.join("; "));
        }
        for note in notes { println!("           {note}"); }
    }
    Ok((passed, failed))
}

/// The keys a case line may carry.
pub const KEYS: [&str; 8] = ["as", "op", "args", "expect", "data", "count", "save", "label"];

// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.3 — an unknown key is refused, never ignored: ignoring it was a false green.
fn validate(case: &Json) -> Result<(), String> {
    let Json::Object(fields) = case else { return Err("each case is a JSON object: {\"op\":..,\"as\":..,\"args\":{..},\"expect\":..}".into()) };
    let valid = KEYS.iter().map(|key| format!("`{key}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    for key in fields.keys() {
        if KEYS.contains(&key.as_str()) { continue; }
        let meant = match key.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
            "rows" | "length" | "len" | "size" | "n" | "total" | "counts" => Some("count"),
            "arguments" | "params" | "parameters" | "input" | "inputs" | "arg" => Some("args"),
            "persona" | "viewer" | "user" | "who" | "principal" => Some("as"),
            "operation" | "query" | "mutation" | "name" => Some("op"),
            "result" | "answer" | "value" | "rowsequal" | "equals" => Some("data"),
            "expected" | "expects" | "outcome" | "state" | "code" | "status" => Some("expect"),
            "description" | "desc" | "comment" | "note" | "title" => Some("label"),
            _ => None,
        };
        let hint = meant.map(|key| format!(" (did you mean `{key}`?)")).unwrap_or_default();
        return Err(format!("unknown key `{key}`{hint}; a case takes only {valid} (snapback4 guide testing)"));
    }
    for key in ["as", "op", "expect", "save", "label"] {
        if let Some(value) = fields.get(key) && !value.is_string() {
            let hint = if key == "expect" && value.is_object() { "; compare the answer with `data` (the whole answer) or `count` (its length) beside `expect`" } else { "" };
            return Err(format!("`{key}` is a string, got {value}{hint}"));
        }
    }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("count") && value.as_u64().is_none() { return Err(format!("`count` is a non-negative integer, got {value}")); }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("args") && !value.is_object() { return Err("\"args\" is an object".into()); }
    Ok(())
}

/// The query's N_NEVER_COMPLETE note, as `N_NEVER_COMPLETE at schema.q:L`.
fn never_complete(bound: &Bound, program: &snapback4_lang::BoundProgram) -> Option<String> {
    bound.notes.iter().chain(&program.notes)
        .find(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") && note.site == program.site)
        .map(|note| format!("{}: {} ({} at {}:{})", program.program.name, note.message, note.code, note.site.file, note.site.line))
}

/// Development evidence under a failing refusal: which layer refused, at which
/// line (LLP 3000.036 §3 B; the refusal itself never names the clause).
fn refusal_evidence(trace: &RefusalTrace, refusal: &Refusal, bound: &Bound, sources: &[(String, String)], explicit: Option<&str>) -> Vec<String> {
    let lookup = |file: &str| sources.iter().find(|(name, _)| name == file).map(|(_, text)| text.clone());
    let cause = trace.take();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    match cause {
        Some(cause) if cause.terminal => {
            let record = super::run_refusal::record(&cause, &bound.refusal_sites);
            if let Some(text) = super::run_refusal::explain(&record, &lookup) { out.push(text.trim_start_matches("dev: ").to_owned()); }
            if refusal.code == "E_RULE" && (cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") || cause.clause.starts_with("cap ")) {
                let held = if cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") { format!("a row with this `{}` key exists that this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) } else { format!("the `{}` group holds rows this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) };
                out.push(format!("hint: {held}; your own code would disclose it, so the engine refuses E_RULE: expect E_RULE here, or make those rows readable to the viewer (guide rules)"));
            }
        }
        // A program's own `! else CODE` or `require … else CODE`, at its
        // source line. When that site is a `t[x] !` lookup and t's read rule
        // hid a row in this operation, the row exists but reads as absent to
        // this viewer: a later `require` never ran.
        cause => if let Some(site) = refusal.site.as_deref().filter(|_| refusal.rule.as_deref() == Some(refusal.code.as_str())) {
            // An engine write-target refusal carries an operation-relative site
            // (`removeSale:2`); resolve it to the source line the program wrote.
            let resolved = operation_site(bound, site);
            let line = super::run_refusal::quote(resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site), &lookup);
            let shown = match (&line, &resolved) {
                (Some(line), Some(at)) => format!("`{line}` ({})", at.trim_end_matches(":1")),
                (Some(line), None) => format!("`{line}` ({site})"),
                (None, _) => site.to_owned(),
            };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — wave 5: a NOT_FOUND the program never names is the write target's answer, not "the program's own refusal".
            if refusal.code == "NOT_FOUND" && !line.as_ref().is_some_and(|line| line.contains("else NOT_FOUND")) {
                out.push(format!("NOT_FOUND is the write target's answer at {shown}: the row is missing, or this viewer cannot read it and the table's write rule refused (a hidden row answers NOT_FOUND, never E_RULE; guide refusals)"));
                return out;
            }
            let hidden = cause.filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule"
                && bound.refusal_sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))));
            match hidden {
                Some(cause) => {
                    let rule = bound.refusal_sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into()));
                    let quoted = rule.map(|rule| match super::run_refusal::quote(rule, &lookup) { Some(text) => format!("`{text}` ({rule})"), None => rule.clone() }).unwrap_or_else(|| "its read rule".into());
                    let later = explicit.filter(|code| *code != refusal.code && sources.iter().any(|(_, text)| text.contains(&format!("else {code}")))).map(|code| format!("; a later `require … else {code}` never ran")).unwrap_or_default();
                    out.push(format!("{} is the program's own refusal at {shown}: the {} read rule {quoted} hid a row from this viewer, and a hidden row reads as absent to a lookup{later}", refusal.code, cause.table));
                    out.push("hint: no code after the lookup can tell \"not yours\" from \"missing\": expect NOT_FOUND here, admit the viewer in the read rule, or write by id with no lookup so the table's update/delete rule decides (snapback4 guide testing, guide refusals)".into());
                }
                None => out.push(format!("{} is the program's own refusal at {shown}", refusal.code)),
            }
        },
    }
    out
}

/// `op:N` (line N of an operation, its header line 1) as `file:line:1`.
fn operation_site(bound: &Bound, site: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let (op, line) = site.rsplit_once(':')?;
    let line: u32 = line.parse().ok()?;
    let program = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op)?;
    Some(format!("{}:{}:1", program.site.file, program.site.line + line.checked_sub(1)?))
}

/// `"$name"` is a saved answer (a saved row's id); `"$name.field"` (any depth) one of its fields.
fn substitute(value: Json, saved: &BTreeMap<String, Json>) -> Result<Json, String> {
    Ok(match value {
        Json::String(text) if text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1 => {
            let mut parts = text[1..].split('.');
            let name = parts.next().unwrap_or_default();
            let mut current = saved.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: nothing was saved as {name} (add \"save\": \"{name}\" to an earlier case)"))?;
            let mut fields = 0;
            for part in parts {
                current = current.get(part).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: the saved answer has no field {part}"))?;
                fields += 1;
            }
            // An argument names a row by its id: a bare `$name` for a saved
            // row (an insert's answer) means that row's id.
            match current.get("id").and_then(Json::as_str) { Some(id) if fields == 0 => Json::String(id.to_owned()), _ => current.clone() }
        }
        Json::Array(items) => Json::Array(items.into_iter().map(|item| substitute(item, saved)).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?),
        Json::Object(fields) => Json::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(k, v)| Ok((k, substitute(v, saved)?))).collect::<Result<_, String>>()?),
        other => other,
    })
}

# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

Deploy into the running serve process without restarting it. A release is
immutable source over one durable store; production admits bearer tokens.

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```

See [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

## Upgrade

`bun add snapback4@latest` upgrades the core package and its platform CLI.
For Expo, use **one command**: `bun add snapback4@latest snapback4-expo@latest`.
The adapter requires the exact core version, including the patch; `check`
warns on stderr if installed versions differ (also with --json). Rebuild your native development/release app
after changing the adapter (Expo Go uses the online tier). Commit the lockfile,
run `bunx snapback4 check`, typecheck the app and run your acceptance tests.
`snapback4 test` uses ./fixtures when present; `--fixture dir` overrides it.

## Deploy and password identity

Add `use identity password` to your schema (the recipe below includes it).
Deploy from the app directory; keep the deployment directory on durable storage:

`serve --port N` accepts 0 for an allocated port or an inclusive range A-B,
choosing its first free port. Invalid input refuses E_INPUT. Stdout's first
line is the actual URL; stderr includes `SNAPBACK4_READY verb=serve … url=…`.
Wait for readiness before requests. For a redeploy, run deploy on the same
host while serve stays running, then verify a read and write with a real
account. Its private owner socket serializes activation; failed validation
keeps the current release. A dev or older unreachable owner requires stopping
that owned process before retrying. See SERVER.md#release-lifecycle.

Layout: `releases/N/` holds each backend; `store.sqlite` holds rows, sessions,
receipts and queues; `assets/` holds uploads; `releases.jsonl` records activation.
`current` is a **text file containing a release number**, not a symlink. The
database's release metadata is authoritative; the file is a convenience copy.
Back up the whole directory with the server and external workers stopped;
include SQLite sidecars, assets and releases. See CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6
for the tested stopped-backup/restore procedure and external-effect caveats.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```

For an executable headless account-seeding recipe, open `guide auth` or `guide headless`.
Use `signup`/`login` from `snapback4/auth` in the app; keep the returned
session in its session store and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
Read the `session` or `denied` body (guide wire): wrong credentials and a
disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. A production persona is 401;
`/seed` and other development doors are 403. Signup creates an account,
not an admin. Sessions survive ordinary restarts and deployments of the same
store until expiry or revocation. A reset/replaced store requires sign-in again;
never deploy by deleting the store to make adoption pass.

## First admin: a service seat

Grant the signed-up account's principal through an operator seat. This recipe
permits ops and existing admins to insert grants, with no public first-user race:

```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```

```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```

Mint on the server host, against the same store. Token stdout is just the token;
prose is stderr. Protect the token as an operator credential. A repeated grant
hits the unique key; this insert-only recipe never takes upsert's update path.
Add revoke/update rules deliberately if your application needs them.

ROLL moved the activation decoder into a SQLite-only module. Remove the stale
web codec census exception for engine/mod.rs::activate; module reachability
already excludes the extracted module, and lifting the gate brings it back
under the scanner. The complete census file reruns 27/27 tests green. No new
codec exemption is introduced.

The fresh GPT review of the Lean correction finds helper-returned references
whose surface type inference is unknown. Emit the named member-type unsupported
reason, following the existing no-guessing rule, rather than a field read on an
id. The engine and real Lean oracle now have an agreeing regression for visible,
hidden and missing direct targets; the equivalent helper engine path is explicitly
unsupported by the emitter. All 9 emitter tests and 7 Lean harness tests pass.
The model and four-signature inherited set remain unchanged. Both rounds of
actual GPT/Muse review responses and their dispositions are preserved verbatim
under llp/reviews/; final independent confirmation is in progress.

## 2026-10-12 — Integration 4 paired code/design confirmation

Fresh, mutually independent GPT-6.1 Sol (high) and Muse Spark 1.3 Contributor
(high) sessions both return READY for aede1021ce. Both inspect release actions,
dormant lifetime invalidation, union/merged acquisition, fixed persona custody,
production identity admission, hot activation traffic/crash tests, database alias
exclusion and the corrected Lean emitter. Muse runs the engine/Lean regression
with SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1. Earlier rounds' findings and missing-inspection
requests are dispositioned without weakening safety or inventing new gates.
The six actual responses (three rounds per family) are verbatim provenance
artifacts in llp/reviews/. These verdicts concern code/design; full qualification
is still running, with no author-acceptance or publication claim inferred.

## 2026-10-12 — Integration 4 qualification completes

The requested five-crate Rust command completes 362 captured summaries
(including a nested subprocess case): 5,686 passed, 5 failed, 41 ignored,
exit 101. Every failure has passing complete-target corrective evidence:
core unit target 306/0/3, CLI friction 19/0/0, ownership/admission 28/0/0,
and guide 119/0/0. The initial guide failure was the corrected introduction's
example placement. Add three passing tests absent from the initial binaries
(the active-reference rollback test and two emitter tests, final emitter 9/9).
Qualified aggregate: **5,694 passed, zero failed, 41 declared ignores**.
The initial command is not called green or rerun totals counted twice.

All 372 regular client files complete: 7,194 passed, 5 failed, 56 skipped.
Four codec census failures are repaired (complete 27/27 rerun). The previously
observed export-background maintenance-drain timeout passes a single unchanged
complete-file rerun: 70 passed, zero failed, 34 declared skips. The five nested
identity-recipe fixture files are included in those 372 files. Qualified regular
aggregate: 7,199 passed. The two serial models run their full 5,000 session
sequences per tier and 3,000 online sequences, adding 81 passed.
Qualified clients: **7,280 passed, zero failed, 56 declared skips**.

Final Lean campaign: 20,000 generated/compiled, 51,024 executions, 213
disagreements only in the four inherited C2/W4 signatures; the signature set
is compared directly with Integration 3. Devices seeds 0–999 keep the same
26 disagreements in 16 inherited signatures. Ordinary run stays 20,000 /
1,096,857 executions / zero disagreements. No new signature or generator
error, and no inherited disagreement is called green. Lean check remains
53 examples, 54 audited standard-axiom theorems and 7 oracle fixtures.
The new engine/oracle reference test and all seven Lean harness tests pass.

Final strict wasm repeats 2,245,422 B raw / 679,418 B gzip; budget 679,918 B
is measurement + 500 B. Client package build passes. Guide deploy prints
150/150 lines (149 source); README is 319/320 and docs-budget passes.
Census regeneration stays 718 entries / 409 mutations / 89 private write sites;
await boundary is unchanged, web codec census scans 169 sources with no error.
Both final blind families are READY. Evidence and exact accounting are in
reports/20261012-integration4/quality-summary.json and its compressed logs.
Final ref-check follows this entry. No push, publication or new root report file.

Ref-check after the qualification entry passes: 541 LLP documents, 11,016 refs,
zero errors and zero unchecked. The tracked worktree is committed without push.

//! `test.cases.jsonl` beside the fixture tables: the application's own
//! operations, in order, as named viewers, each with the outcome it expects.
//! One line is `{"as": "alice", "op": "addCard", "args": {..}, "expect": "sent"}`;
//! `expect` is `sent` (a mutation's default), `complete`, `capped`, `ok` (any
//! answer), or a refusal code (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, a program's own `require`
//! code). A query's default is its `check` verdict: `complete`, or either
//! answer when `check` notes the query is never complete (N_NEVER_COMPLETE).
//! Optional `data` must equal the answer; `count` is the answer's length; `save`
//! names the answer so a later string argument `"$name"` or `"$name.field"`
//! reuses it. `as` is a declared persona, as on dev: an undeclared one is
//! refused E_AUTH.
use crate::project::persona_principal;
use snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace;
use snapback4_core::ir::ProgramKind;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, WriteState, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

pub const FILE: &str = "test.cases.jsonl";

/// Dev's cast check, for `test` and a local `run`: with `use personas`
/// declared, a bare name (or `dev:<name>`) outside the cast is refused as dev
/// refuses its header (E_AUTH); a principal id such as `user:…` passes as itself.
pub fn undeclared_persona(name: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Option<String> {
    if declared.is_empty() { return None; }
    let bare = match name.split_once(':') { None => name, Some(("dev", bare)) => bare, Some(_) => return None };
    (!declared.iter().any(|p| p == bare)).then(|| format!(
        "persona {bare} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast, and dev refuses {bare} with E_AUTH (401). Add {bare} to it, or use a declared persona (an outsider is a declared persona with no rows)",
        declared.join(", ")))
}

/// The persona a case without `as` runs as: alice when she is in the cast (or
/// there is none), else the first declared persona.
pub fn default_persona(declared: &[String]) -> &str {
    if declared.is_empty() || declared.iter().any(|p| p == "alice") { "alice" } else { &declared[0] }
}

/// One case as `test`'s query sweep reads it: which query, as whom, with
/// which arguments (`None` when they name a saved answer, which only the case
/// run has), expecting what.
pub struct Explicit { pub at: String, pub op: String, pub principal: String, pub args: Option<Json>, pub expect: Option<String> }

/// The cases file read leniently, before the sweep: a line that does not
/// parse is left to [`run`], which refuses it with its line number.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: the sweep runs a persona's own case arguments and labels a refusal its case expects.
pub fn explicit(fixture: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Vec<Explicit> {
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(fixture).join(FILE)) else { return Vec::new() };
    fn saved(value: &Json) -> bool {
        match value { Json::String(text) => text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1, Json::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), Json::Object(fields) => fields.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    }
    text.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(index, line)| {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { return None; }
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).ok()?;
        let op = case.get("op")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(declared));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        let args = Some(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}))).filter(|args| args.is_object() && !saved(args));
        Some(Explicit { at: format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1), op, principal, args, expect: case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str).map(str::to_owned) })
    }).collect()
}

/// Runs every case; prints one line each and returns (passed, failed).
/// `sources` are the compiled files, to quote a refusing clause's line.
pub fn run(engine: &mut Engine, bound: &Bound, fixture: &str, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let path = Path::new(fixture).join(FILE);
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
        Ok(text) => text,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((0, 0)),
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display())),
    };
    let mut saved = BTreeMap::<String, Json>::new();
    let (mut passed, mut failed) = (0, 0);
    let mut verdicts_shown = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let lines: Vec<(usize, &str)> = text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.trim_start().starts_with("//")).collect();
    // Every line is checked before any runs: a misspelled key must not pass
    // silently (wave 5: `"rows":0` passed against one row), and a typo on the
    // last line should not wait for every earlier case.
    let mut cases = Vec::with_capacity(lines.len());
    for (index, line) in &lines {
        let at = format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1);
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
        validate(&case).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        cases.push((at, case));
    }
    println!("test: cases from {FILE}");
    for (at, case) in cases {
        let Some(op) = case.get("op").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return Err(format!("{at}: each case names \"op\" (a query or mutation)")) };
        let Some(bound_program) = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op) else {
            let starter = super::init::starter_fixture(FILE).is_some_and(|starter| starter == text);
            let hint = if starter { format!("\n  hint: {FILE} is the todo starter's unmodified cases for list/add/toggle/remove; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it (write your own cases, `snapback4 guide testing`)") } else { String::new() };
            return Err(format!("{at}: unknown operation {op}{hint}"));
        };
        let program = bound_program.program.clone();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(&bound.personas));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        // A query's default expectation is check's verdict: a noted never-complete page passes capped.
        let never_complete = (program.kind == ProgramKind::Query).then(|| never_complete(bound, bound_program)).flatten();
        let explicit = case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str);
        let expect = explicit.unwrap_or(match program.kind { ProgramKind::Query if never_complete.is_some() => "ok", ProgramKind::Query => "complete", _ => "sent" });
        let label = case.get("label").and_then(Json::as_str).map(|l| format!(" — {l}")).unwrap_or_default();
        let raw_args = substitute(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({})), &saved).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
        if let Json::Object(fields) = &raw_args {
            for (name, value) in fields {
                let Some(kind) = program.args.get(name) else { return Err(format!("{at}: {op} takes no argument {name}")) };
                args.insert(name.clone(), wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: argument {name}: {why}"))?);
            }
        } else { return Err(format!("{at}: \"args\" is an object")); }
        let trace = RefusalTrace::default();
        let context = Context::new(principal, args).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
        // (outcome word, answer) or a refusal
        let mut unavailable = false;
        let got: Result<(&str, Json), Refusal> = if let Some(why) = undeclared_persona(persona, &bound.personas) {
            Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", why))
        } else { match program.kind {
            ProgramKind::Query => match engine.query(op, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => { unavailable = outcome.unavailable; Ok((if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" }, wire::to_json(&outcome.data))) },
                Err(refusal) => Err(refusal),
            },
            ProgramKind::Job => return Err(format!("{at}: {op} is a job; cases run queries and mutations")),
            _ => match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                    WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => Ok(("sent", wire::to_json(&result))),
                    WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => Err(why),
                },
                Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
            },
        } };
        let mut problems = Vec::new();
        let mut notes = Vec::new();
        let shown = match &got {
            Ok((word, answer)) => {
                let admitted = *word == expect || expect == "ok" || (expect == "complete" && *word == "sent");
                if !admitted { problems.push(if expect == "capped" && *word == "complete" {
                    "now complete — update the case: replace expect: \"capped\" with expect: \"complete\" (or omit expect); readable pinned-parent guards can now certify this page".to_owned()
                } else { format!("expected {expect}, got {word}") }); }
                if *word == "capped" && let Some(note) = &never_complete {
                    if admitted && explicit.is_none() && verdicts_shown.insert(op.to_owned()) { notes.push(format!("capped is this query's check verdict: {note}")); }
                    if !admitted { notes.push(format!("hint: {note}; a capped answer still holds every row you may read: add \"expect\":\"capped\" (or drop \"expect\"), or pin the page as the note's rewrite says (snapback4 check --verbose)")); }
                }
                if let Some(data) = case.get("data") && data != answer {
                    problems.push(format!("data differs: got {answer}"));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.12 — an unavailable answer is not a null value: say why it has none.
                    if unavailable { notes.push("null here is no answer (a client shows `loading`), not a null value: a ceiling cut it, or an `exists` over rows this viewer only partly reads found no row it may read, which cannot prove absence (snapback4 check --verbose)".into()); }
                }
                if let Some(count) = case.get("count").and_then(Json::as_u64) {
                    let length = answer.as_array().map(|a| a.len()).or_else(|| answer.get("items").and_then(Json::as_array).map(|a| a.len()));
                    if length != Some(count as usize) { problems.push(format!("expected {count} rows, got {}", length.map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| "a non-list".into()))); }
                }
                if let Some(name) = case.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) { saved.insert(name.to_owned(), answer.clone()); }
                (*word).to_owned()
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                let code = &refusal.code;
                if code != expect && !(expect == "denied" || expect == "refused" || expect == "failed") {
                    problems.push(format!("expected {expect}, got {code}: {}", refusal.message));
                    notes.extend(refusal_evidence(&trace, refusal, bound, sources, explicit));
                }
                code.clone()
            }
        };
        if problems.is_empty() {
            passed += 1;
            println!("      ok   {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}");
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            println!("      FAIL {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}: {}", problems.join("; "));
        }
        for note in notes { println!("           {note}"); }
    }
    Ok((passed, failed))
}

/// The keys a case line may carry.
pub const KEYS: [&str; 8] = ["as", "op", "args", "expect", "data", "count", "save", "label"];

// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.3 — an unknown key is refused, never ignored: ignoring it was a false green.
fn validate(case: &Json) -> Result<(), String> {
    let Json::Object(fields) = case else { return Err("each case is a JSON object: {\"op\":..,\"as\":..,\"args\":{..},\"expect\":..}".into()) };
    let valid = KEYS.iter().map(|key| format!("`{key}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    for key in fields.keys() {
        if KEYS.contains(&key.as_str()) { continue; }
        let meant = match key.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
            "rows" | "length" | "len" | "size" | "n" | "total" | "counts" => Some("count"),
            "arguments" | "params" | "parameters" | "input" | "inputs" | "arg" => Some("args"),
            "persona" | "viewer" | "user" | "who" | "principal" => Some("as"),
            "operation" | "query" | "mutation" | "name" => Some("op"),
            "result" | "answer" | "value" | "rowsequal" | "equals" => Some("data"),
            "expected" | "expects" | "outcome" | "state" | "code" | "status" => Some("expect"),
            "description" | "desc" | "comment" | "note" | "title" => Some("label"),
            _ => None,
        };
        let hint = meant.map(|key| format!(" (did you mean `{key}`?)")).unwrap_or_default();
        return Err(format!("unknown key `{key}`{hint}; a case takes only {valid} (snapback4 guide testing)"));
    }
    for key in ["as", "op", "expect", "save", "label"] {
        if let Some(value) = fields.get(key) && !value.is_string() {
            let hint = if key == "expect" && value.is_object() { "; compare the answer with `data` (the whole answer) or `count` (its length) beside `expect`" } else { "" };
            return Err(format!("`{key}` is a string, got {value}{hint}"));
        }
    }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("count") && value.as_u64().is_none() { return Err(format!("`count` is a non-negative integer, got {value}")); }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("args") && !value.is_object() { return Err("\"args\" is an object".into()); }
    Ok(())
}

/// The query's N_NEVER_COMPLETE note, as `N_NEVER_COMPLETE at schema.q:L`.
fn never_complete(bound: &Bound, program: &snapback4_lang::BoundProgram) -> Option<String> {
    bound.notes.iter().chain(&program.notes)
        .find(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") && note.site == program.site)
        .map(|note| format!("{}: {} ({} at {}:{})", program.program.name, note.message, note.code, note.site.file, note.site.line))
}
// The index card (LLP 3000 §2; LLP 2000.000 §8). These are the only words a
// screen may need. The mechanism is built to fit them; a screen that needs a
// word not here is a defect in the mechanism, not a gap in the card.

export interface Session {
  readonly principal: string;
  /** `guest` or `user` for built-in identity; an application-defined kind
   * for a session minted by its service. */
  readonly kind: string;
  readonly token: string;
  readonly expiresAt: number;
  /** Absent on legacy persisted sessions; authority always comes from the server. */
  readonly scope?: "full" | "read";
  readonly capExpiresAt?: number;
}

export type SignIn = { readonly ok: true; readonly session: Session } | { readonly ok: false; readonly why: Refusal };
export type SessionRevocation = { readonly ok: true; readonly revoked: number } | { readonly ok: false; readonly why: Refusal };

/** An opaque page position minted by whoever evaluated the page. Pass it back unchanged. */
export type Cursor = string & { readonly __cursor: "Cursor" };
/** The authenticated subject a rule sees as `viewer`. */
export type Principal = string & { readonly __principal: "Principal" };

/** Why something was refused. `family` and `code` are stable; `rewrite` compiles. */
export interface Refusal {
  readonly code: string;
  readonly family: string;
  readonly message: string;
  /** Original constraint text for development; render message to the user. */
  readonly detail?: string;
  readonly site?: string;
  readonly rewrite?: string;
  readonly guide?: string;
  readonly rule?: string;
  readonly retryable?: boolean;
}

export type ReadCut = "meter" | "words" | null;

/**
 * A read. `complete` is false beyond a sync horizon or at an online-only site.
 * `cut` names a disclosed meter or word-limit cut. Non-uniform server reads
 * withhold it: null means "not disclosed here", not "not cut".
 * An empty meter-cut page is partial: retry narrower, or continue if `next` exists.
 * `fresh` is transport truth: the value reflects the server now, as far as the
 * change feed can tell. When it does not, `since` is the wall-clock time (ms) of
 * the newest server state it does reflect, so a screen can say "as of three minutes ago".
 * Observations discard responses older than observed commits in the same store and
 * permanently refuse on store replacement or regression.
 * `connected[p]` is the exception. No commit announces that `connected[p]` changed, so an answer of a query that
 * may read it is a sample: it keeps the `fresh` it arrived with (true for a server's whole answer, false when
 * a local answer composes retained rows) until a newer answer replaces it. A background re-read about
 * 5 seconds after each reply settles does that while refreshes succeed; failed refreshes or a suspended
 * client leave the sample on screen, with the same flag, and no bound on its age.
 * A mounted same-key observation retains its answer during plain revalidation,
 * marked `revalidating: true`, until replacement, with no timeout. Transport
 * failure marks `fresh: false`; retry exhaustion also keeps the answer. Local
 * commits, durable/ephemeral change evidence and /sync publications start one
 * non-renewable second at the replacement attempt; capacity queue wait pauses
 * this budget. Online change
 * evidence can be revocation, so its second starts on receipt and includes queue
 * wait. Failure or timeout withdraws; a later accepted answer can still publish.
 * Applied local scope loss withdraws dependent audience groups immediately,
 * including staged removals; older replica answers wait for publication.
 * `keep: false` withdraws immediately; `keep: true` adds a 30 s subscriber-free
 * linger. Gate actions on `isReady(read) && !read.revalidating` when they
 * require a completed re-read.
 * Every option withdraws on expiry, a refusal, a viewer or credential change,
 * store reset, adoption or close. Retention never grants authority.
 * @ref LLP 3000.023#10 — all change evidence bounds retention.
 * Before the first answer use `loading`; a refusal carries its message in `denied`.
 */
// @ref LLP 3000.031#1.1 — existence describes the disclosed value, never hidden rows.
export interface ReadyMetadata {
  readonly complete: boolean;
  readonly cut: ReadCut;
  readonly fresh: boolean;
  readonly revalidating?: true;
  readonly since?: number;
  readonly next?: Cursor | null;
  readonly meter?: ReadMeter;
}
// @ref LLP 3000.031#3 — named arms preserve generic covariance.
// Every arm retains T; absent additionally proves null or an empty array.
interface Present<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "present";
  readonly data: T & ({} | undefined);
}
interface Partial<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {

use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}
        })
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleExpr {
    Public { reason: String },
    Allow,
    Deny,
    Eq(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    Ne(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    /// Ordered comparisons are confined to named update transitions and to
    /// insert rules over the row's own required int/time columns.
    Compare(crate::ir::BinaryOp, RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    And(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Or(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Not(Box<RuleExpr>),
    Exists {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
    /// True only when the indexed target row was inserted earlier in this
    /// same transaction; insert rules only.
    Created {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn is_public(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. })
    }

    pub fn is_uniformly_readable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow)
    }

    /// Every `.field` the rule reads, on any probe key or comparison.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
            if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } = term {
                out.insert(field.clone());
            }
        };
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow | Self::Deny => {}
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    }

    pub(super) fn upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| runner.upsert_attempt(table_name, key, row))
    }

    fn upsert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row)?;
        let id = key.as_str()?;
        if row.field("id")?.as_str()? != id {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} upsert row id must equal its key")));
        }
        self.reserve_target_resolution(table_name, self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?, &row, true)?;
        match self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.fetch(allowance, table_name, id))? {
            Some(old) => {
                let readable = self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
                let row = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, row, readable)?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — an existing row receives a patch; omitted columns keep their values.
                let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &row, readable)?;
                self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &row, copy)
            }
            None if self.device && !self.upsert_absence_proven(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])? => Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])),
            None => self.insert_upsert(table_name, "byId", row),
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, addressed_index: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            // A read-through child's insert arm keeps the hidden-target word for every authority refusal.
            return self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()).map_err(|e| match e {
                RuntimeError::Rule {..} | RuntimeError::Input(_) => self.no_write_target(), other => other,
            });
        }
        match self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()) {
            Err(RuntimeError::Input(_)) => Err(self.no_write_target()),
            result => result,
        }
    }

    fn replace(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value, new: Value, readable: bool, patch: &Value, copy: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.authorize_update_fields(table_name, &old, &new, patch, readable)?;
        self.replace_authorized(table_name, old, new, readable, patch, copy)
    }

    /// `copy` is the merged image's lawful price, charged again for an engine slot copy.
    pub(super) fn replace_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value, new: Value, readable: bool, patch: &Value, copy: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, patch, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, patch)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, patch)?;
        self.authorize_fields(table_name, &old, patch)?;

// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve before arm selection; resolve live, stamp, then authorize.
use super::{rules, runner::Runner};
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, Table, through::*},
    value::{Supplied, Value},
};

/// Minted only here: the stamped image re-enters the supplied-input channel
/// under reserve_capture's declared allowance.
pub(crate) struct CaptureInput(());

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum ParentVerdict {
    Readable,
    Unreadable,
    Absent,
    Unknown,
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn reserve_capture(&mut self, table: &Table) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        if self.capture_prepaid {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        self.charge_price(crate::schema::reservations::capture_price(self.schema, table))?;
        self.capture_prepaid = true;
        self.read_tables.insert(through.parent.clone());
        self.rule_dependencies(&parent.rules.read);
        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.insert);
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn capture_insert(&mut self, name: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let result = self.capture_insert_inner(name, row);
        self.trace_authorization(&result, name, Effect::Insert, "read through capture");
        result
    }

    fn capture_insert_inner(
        &mut self,
        name: &str,
        row: Supplied,
    ) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(row);
        };
        let mut row = row.into_value();
        let id = row
            .field(&through.reference)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
            .ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent reference"))?;
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the parent lookup is authorization work under the
        // request's physical allowance; reserve_capture prepaid its logical charges.
        let found = self.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.get(scope, &through.parent, id))?.filter(|r| !self.expired(parent, r));
        if self.device {
            let covered = if let Some(found) = &found {
                self.is_provisional(&through.parent, found)? || self.device_work(|runner| {
                    let viewer = runner.context.viewer.clone();
                    let now = runner.context.now.unwrap_or(0);
                    runner.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.recording_covered(&through.parent, found, &viewer, now, scope, &mut runner.meter))
                })?
            } else { false };
            if !covered { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("read through .{} needs a live, covered {} parent; queued without a row", through.reference, through.parent))); }
        }
        let found = found.ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent missing or expired"))?;
        stamp(parent, through, &found, &mut row)?;
        table.recompute_deadline(&mut row)?;
        if !self.context.is_system() {
            let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
            let readable = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[], |runner, allowance| {
                rules::evaluate(&table.rules.read, &viewer, None, &row, false, &mut super::read::PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
            })?;
            if !readable {
                return Err(self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent read rule"));
            }
        }
        Ok(row.capture_input(&CaptureInput(())))
    }

    pub(super) fn physical_children(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let children: Vec<_> = self
            .schema
            .children(parent)
            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())
            .collect();
        // Reserve the complete schema-fixed probe set before its first seek.
        // Never short-circuit on an occupied table, including at low ceilings.

        let heap = children.iter().map(|name| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[name])).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::Steps, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
            let mut occupied = false;

                occupied |= !runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?.is_empty();
            }
            Ok(occupied)
        })
    }

            .schema

//! Rule evaluation and the static facts about it that the engine hoists: a
//! rule that is proven true by the scanned prefix (`decided_true`) costs no
//! probes; a rule whose deciding fields are pinned by the prefix (`uniform`) is
//! decided once for the whole range. Copied from Snapback 2's uniformity
//! analysis (LLP 2000.000 §5.1); the certificate that priced these is gone,
//! the analysis is not.

use crate::ir::{Expr, IndexKeyExpr, Input, Merge, Scan};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema, Table};
use crate::value::Value;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Visibility {
    /// One verdict serves every row of the scanned prefix.
    pub uniform: bool,
    /// That verdict is `true` before any row is read.
    pub decided_true: bool,
}

pub fn scan_visibility(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Visibility {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten());
    visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned)

}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — a missing upsert may insert only when the complete held group and its key decide that no hidden target can exist.
pub fn upsert_absence(schema: &Schema, name: &str, index: &str, key: &[Expr]) -> Result<Visibility, &'static str> {
    let table = schema.table(name).ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    let sync = table.sync.as_ref().ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    if sync.horizon.is_some() { return Err("the target has a horizon"); }
    let rule = table.rules.read.device_audience_rule();
    // Public subset sync has no whole-table absence proof. Its group coverage
    // is a separate decision; do not infer it merely from the public read rule.
    if rule.is_uniformly_readable() && sync.audience != crate::schema::Audience::Public { return Err("the key's synced group is not established"); }
    let index = table.index(index).ok_or("the key does not pin readability")?;
    let pinned = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(key).filter_map(|(part, expression)| part.column().map(|column| (column, expression))));
    let visibility = prefix_visibility(name, table, &rule, &pinned);
    if !visibility.uniform { return Err("the key does not pin readability"); }
    Ok(visibility)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — simplify self-witnesses, then pre-check only the leading conjuncts whose fields the scope pins, in evaluation order.
pub(super) fn prefix_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    let index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
    let pinned: BTreeMap<_,_> = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())));
    if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| pinned.contains_key(&t.reference)) { return Some(table.rules.read.clone()); }
    let rule = simplify_prefix(&table.rules.read, &scan.table, table, &pinned);
    if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Allow) { return None; }
    let mut terms = Vec::new();
    conjuncts(rule, &mut terms);
    let decided: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().take_while(|term| {
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        term.fields(&mut fields);
        fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(field))
    }).collect();
    (!decided.is_empty()).then_some(RuleExpr::And(decided))
}

// Flatten only conjunctions, preserving left-to-right short-circuit order.
fn conjuncts(rule: RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<RuleExpr>) {
    match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => for term in terms { conjuncts(term, out); }, rule => out.push(rule) }
}

fn simplify_prefix(rule: &RuleExpr, name: &str, table: &Table, pinned: &BTreeMap<&String, &Expr>) -> RuleExpr {
    if rule_true(rule, name, table, &|name| pinned.iter().find_map(|(key, value)| (key.as_str() == name).then_some(*value))) { return RuleExpr::Allow; }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for term in terms { conjuncts(simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned), &mut out); }
            out.retain(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Allow));
            RuleExpr::And(out)
        },
        RuleExpr::Or(_) => {
            // Use the same self-witness absorption as uniformity, but remove
            // the absorbed field read from the expression we actually run.
            let terms = rule.disjuncts();
            RuleExpr::Or(terms.iter().filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| self_probe_absorbs(name, table, term, other))).map(|term| simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned)).collect())
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(simplify_prefix(inner, name, table, pinned))),
        _ => rule.clone(),
    }
}

/// A read rule `A or G` whose disjunct `G` reads no column of the row (a
/// role such as `exists staff[viewer]`, or a service seat) is true for every
/// row whenever `G` holds for this caller. The page is then decided once, per
/// caller: a staff console's list is complete for staff, and is the same
/// per-row walk as before for everyone else.
///
/// Only grants whose every fact the caller could read are used, so `complete`
/// discloses nothing beyond the caller's own readable rows: each probe's
/// target row must be readable by its own read rule once the probe key pins
/// it (`.principal = viewer`, `public`, or the same probe), and a negated
/// probe is never a grant.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — the role-guard completeness proof deferred in §11, server-side and per caller.
pub fn grant_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, false)
}

/// The disjuncts of `scan`'s read rule that decide the page for a caller they
/// admit: the readable grants (`grant_decision`), and each disjunct whose every
/// row column the scan's prefix pins and whose probed rows its holders can
/// read (`pinned_probe_readable`), such as `exists boards[.board, owner =
/// viewer]` under `.board = b`. The page walk (`read.rs`) evaluates them once,
/// with the prefix's values for the pinned columns: when one holds, every row
/// the scan can visit is readable, so the page is decided as a uniform page
/// is. Each verdict reads only rows the caller may read, so `complete`
/// discloses nothing beyond them.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.3 — a parent probe the pins decide is a page guard, as a grant is.
pub fn page_guard_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, true)
}

fn guard_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan, pinned_guards: bool) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    if table.through.is_some() { return None; }
    let rule = &table.rules.read;
    if !matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Or(_)) { return None; }
    let pinned = if pinned_guards { scan_pinned(table, scan) } else { Vec::new() };
    let guards: Vec<RuleExpr> = rule.disjuncts().into_iter()
        .filter(|disjunct| {
            let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
            disjunct.fields(&mut fields);
            if fields.is_empty() { self_disclosing(schema, disjunct) }
            else { fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.iter().any(|(column, _)| *column == field)) && pinned_probe_readable(schema, disjunct) }
        })
        .cloned().collect();
    (!guards.is_empty()).then(|| if guards.len() == 1 { guards.into_iter().next().unwrap() } else { RuleExpr::Or(guards) })
}

/// The columns the scan's prefix pins, with their value expressions.
fn scan_pinned<'s>(table: &'s Table, scan: &'s Scan) -> Vec<(&'s String, &'s Expr)> {
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Vec::new() };
    index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())).collect()
}

/// Every caller who reaches `scan` holds the scan's readable grant, in a
/// program that never writes the grant's table (so the fact cannot change
/// between the guard and the walk): the page is fully readable for each of
/// them, which is exactly when the server's walk (`read.rs`) answers
/// `complete`. The guards followed are the ones authors write:
///
/// - `require G else …` before the statement holding the scan;
/// - `if G:` around it (and the `else` of `if not G:`);
/// - an early exit, `if not G: return …` (or `if viewer != 'service:x':
///   return []`), before it.
///
/// `G` may be an `exists` probe (with the grant's named equalities, as
/// `exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager']` lowers them) or a
/// `viewer = '…'` comparison, and may be one conjunct of a larger condition.
/// A page guard on a pinned column (`exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]`
/// under `.board = b`) is implied by the same probe of the pinned value
/// (`exists boards[b, owner = viewer]`) when that value is a literal,
/// `viewer` or an argument the program never rebinds.
/// Scans compare structurally, so every copy of the scan must be guarded.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — require dominance: staff consoles and staff-only mutation passes.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#13.1 — `if` guards and early exits dominate too, so `check` agrees with the runtime verdict.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.3 — a guard on the pinned parent probe dominates as a grant's does.
pub fn grant_dominated(schema: &Schema, program: &crate::ir::Program, scan: &Scan) -> bool {
    let Some(grant) = page_guard_decision(schema, scan) else { return false };
    let written: BTreeSet<String> = crate::ir::write_tables(program).into_iter().map(|(table, _)| table).collect();
    let mut probes = grant_probe_bindings(&program.body);
    probes.retain(|_, read| !written.contains(&read.table));
    let conditions = short_circuit_bindings(&program.body);
    let mut rebound = BTreeMap::new();
    assignment_counts(&program.body, &mut rebound);
    let mut pins = schema.table(&scan.table).map(|table| scan_pinned(table, scan)).unwrap_or_default();
    pins.retain(|(_, value)| match value {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Input(Input::Viewer) => true,
        Expr::Variable(name) => program.args.contains_key(name) && !rebound.contains_key(name),
        _ => false,
    });
    let guard = Guard { grant: &grant, written: &written, probes: &probes, conditions: &conditions, pins: &pins };
    let mut walk = Dominance { scan, found: 0, guarded: 0 };
    walk.body(&guard, &program.body, false);
    walk.found > 0 && walk.found == walk.guarded
}

/// `name = t[key]` bindings the compiler makes for a probe with named
/// equalities (`$probe1 = people[viewer]`; `$probe1 != null and
/// $probe1.role = 'manager'`). A name bound twice or reassigned is dropped.
fn grant_probe_bindings(body: &[crate::ir::Stmt]) -> BTreeMap<String, crate::ir::Read> {
    use crate::ir::Stmt;
    fn collect(body: &[Stmt], out: &mut BTreeMap<String, Option<crate::ir::Read>>) {
        for statement in body {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Const { name, value } => {
                    let read = if let Expr::Get(read) = value { Some(read.clone()) } else { None };
                    if out.insert(name.clone(), read).is_some() { out.insert(name.clone(), None); }
                }
                Stmt::Assign { name, .. } => { out.insert(name.clone(), None); }
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { collect(then_body, out); collect(else_body, out); }
                Stmt::For { binding, body, .. } => { out.insert(binding.clone(), None); collect(body, out); }
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
    collect(body, &mut out);
    out.into_iter().filter_map(|(name, read)| read.map(|read| (name, read))).collect()
}

/// How often each name is bound or assigned anywhere in `body` (an argument
/// that appears here no longer holds the value the scan's prefix was
/// evaluated from; a short-circuit temporary is assigned exactly twice).
fn assignment_counts(body: &[crate::ir::Stmt], out: &mut BTreeMap<String, usize>) {
    use crate::ir::Stmt;
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Let { name, .. } | Stmt::Const { name, .. } | Stmt::Assign { name, .. } => *out.entry(name.clone()).or_default() += 1,
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { assignment_counts(then_body, out); assignment_counts(else_body, out); }
            Stmt::For { binding, body, .. } => { *out.entry(binding.clone()).or_default() += 1; assignment_counts(body, out); }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

/// The compiler lowers a short-circuit `a and b` (`a or b`) to a temporary:
/// `$bool1 = a`, then `if $bool1: $bool1 = b` (`if not $bool1: …`, or
/// `if $bool1:` with the assignment in its `else`). Each such
/// temporary, assigned only there, stands for the whole condition.
fn short_circuit_bindings(body: &[crate::ir::Stmt]) -> BTreeMap<String, Expr> {
    use crate::ir::{BinaryOp, Stmt, UnaryOp};
    fn collect(body: &[Stmt], counts: &BTreeMap<String, usize>, out: &mut BTreeMap<String, Expr>) {
        for pair in body.windows(2) {
            let [Stmt::Let { name, value: first }, Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body }] = pair else { continue };
            if !name.starts_with('$') || counts.get(name) != Some(&2) { continue; }
            // `if $b: $b = second` (and), `if not $b: $b = second` or
            // `if $b: (nothing) else: $b = second` (or).
            let tested = |negated: bool| match condition {
                Expr::Variable(tested) => !negated && tested == name,
                Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Not, value } => negated && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(tested) if tested == name),
                _ => false,
            };
            let (op, body) = if else_body.is_empty() && tested(false) { (BinaryOp::And, then_body) }
                else if else_body.is_empty() && tested(true) { (BinaryOp::Or, then_body) }
                else if then_body.is_empty() && tested(false) { (BinaryOp::Or, else_body) }
                else { continue };
            // The second operand's own setup (a probe's `$probe2 = t[key]`) precedes the assignment.
            let Some((Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: second }, setup)) = body.split_last() else { continue };
            if !setup.iter().all(|statement| matches!(statement, Stmt::Let { .. } | Stmt::Const { .. })) { continue; }
