  readonly since?: number;
  readonly next?: Cursor | null;
  readonly meter?: ReadMeter;
}
// @ref LLP 3000.031#3 — named arms preserve generic covariance.
// Every arm retains T; absent additionally proves null or an empty array.
interface Present<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "present";
  readonly data: T & ({} | undefined);
}
interface Partial<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "partial";
  readonly data: T;
}
interface Absent<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "absent";
  readonly data: T & (null | []);
  readonly complete: true;
  readonly cut: null;
}
export type Ready<T> = Present<T> | Partial<T> | Absent<T>;
export type Read<T> =
  | Ready<T>
  | { readonly loading: true }
  | { readonly denied: Refusal };
/** A one-shot's final answer; ready can still be partial or stale. */
export type SettledRead<T> = Exclude<Read<T>, { readonly loading: true }>;

/** Engine work for this server answer under dev; never returned by serve. */
export interface ReadMeter {
  /** Precise reason, including non-uniform work that public `Read.cut` withholds. */
  readonly cut: ReadCut;
  readonly examined: number;
  readonly indexEntries: number;
  readonly probes: number;
  readonly steps: number;
  readonly heapBytes: number;
  readonly outputBytes: number;
  readonly rowsWritten: number;
}

/** A row this device changed and the server has not yet confirmed carries `pending: true`. */
export interface Pending {
  readonly pending?: boolean;
}

/** A write. Fresh `sent` carries the server answer, including null. A replay
 * confirms execution identity only: `replayed: true` has no historical result.
 * Pending rows show predictions; `id` is caller-supplied or client-minted and stable across replay.
 * A prediction can still end `failed`: an insert predicts without proving its caller-supplied id or
 * any unique-key value absent, so a row this device cannot read with that id or value makes the
 * server refuse `E_RULE` and settlement withdraws the pending row. Row ids minted for the call never
 * collide this way; another unique value can. */
// @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — replay confirms execution without historical read authority
export type Write<R = unknown> =
  | { readonly state: "pending"; readonly id: string; readonly reason?: string }
  | { readonly state: "sent"; readonly id: string; readonly seq: number; readonly replayed?: false; readonly result: R }
  | { readonly state: "sent"; readonly id: string; readonly seq: number; readonly replayed: true; readonly result?: never }
  | { readonly state: "failed"; readonly id: string; readonly why: Refusal };

/** An admitted intent, oldest first. `sent` here means dispatch began and the
 * outcome is unknown; only Write.sent is a receipt. Held reasons are refusal
 * codes; predictions name rows as `table/id`, never row contents. */
// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.3 — an outbox list also represents writes without predicted rows.
export interface PendingWrite<A = unknown> {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly op: string;
  readonly args: A;
  readonly state: "queued" | "sent" | "held";
  readonly reason?: string;
  readonly predicted: readonly string[];
}

/** An action's server outcome. A denial precedes admission; a pending id
 * may already be running. Retry with that same id, never predict or queue
 * an action on the device. This new card word needs the author's ruling. */
// @ref LLP 3000.007#9.2 — request/response worker results have no predicted state.
export type Act<R = unknown> =
  | { readonly state: "done"; readonly id: string; readonly result: R }
  | { readonly state: "failed"; readonly id: string; readonly why: Refusal }
  | { readonly state: "pending"; readonly id: string }

import { registerWebWitness } from "snapback4/witness-web";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
import { Platform } from "react-native";
import * as SecureStore from "expo-secure-store";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { nativeAvailable, nativeDevice } from "snapback4-expo";
import { smokeOrigin } from "snapback4-expo/witness";
import { webDevice } from "snapback4/web";

// The backend `snapback4 dev` serves; the iOS simulator reaches this machine's
// loopback. Set EXPO_PUBLIC_SNAPBACK_URL to your TLS deployment for a phone.
export const url = smokeOrigin((Platform.OS === "web" ? new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("api") : null) ?? process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_SNAPBACK_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:4400");

// Development and production builds carry the native device (offline data and
// a durable outbox); Expo Go has no native module and uses the online client;
// web uses the wasm device.
export const tier = Platform.OS === "web" ? "web" : nativeAvailable() ? "native" : "online";

const sessionStore = {
  get: (key: string) => SecureStore.getItemAsync(key),
  set: (key: string, value: string) => SecureStore.setItemAsync(key, value),
  delete: (key: string) => SecureStore.deleteItemAsync(key),
};
// Browser storage is readable by any script on the page; SecureStore is not.
export const app = createApp({
  url,
  sessionStore: Platform.OS === "web" ? localStorageSessionStore() : sessionStore,
  device: tier === "web" ? webDevice() : tier === "native" ? nativeDevice : undefined,
});

registerWebWitness(app, api);

# Client: answers stay on screen

**Start here:** `useQuery`/`usePage`, `matchRead`/`readPresentation`; composers/receipts above rows. `useFollowing` (`guide feeds`).

```tsx
import { matchRead, readPresentation, usePage } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Inbox() { const page = usePage(api.inboxCards, {}), status = readPresentation(page.read); return <>{matchRead(page.read, { loading: () => "Loading…", denied: why => why.message,
    absent: () => "No conversations", partial: () => "Partial results", present: rows => `${rows.length} conversations` })}
    {status.incomplete && "Results may be incomplete"}{page.items.map(row => <p key={row.id}>{row.title} ({row.memberCount})</p>)}<button disabled={!page.hasMore} onClick={page.loadMore}>More</button></>;
}
```

- `E_OFFLINE`: unavailable; reconnect.
- `E_QUERY_RETRY`: settlement exhausted; retry.

## Reference
```tsx
import { useState } from "react"; import { matchRead, useMutation, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Composer({ conversationId }: { conversationId: string }) {
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState(""); const send = useMutation(api.send);
  const member = useQuery(api.myMembership, { conversationId });
  const blocked = matchRead(member, { denied: () => true, absent: read => read.fresh && !read.revalidating, partial: () => false, otherwise: () => false });
  const [submitted, setSubmitted] = useState("");
  async function submit() {
    if (blocked) return;
    const text = draft; setSubmitted(text); setDraft(""); const write = await send.run({ conversationId, body: text }, { waitSeconds: 0 });
    if (write.state === "failed") setDraft(current => current || text);
  }
  return <><input value={draft} onChange={e => setDraft(e.target.value)} />
    <button disabled={send.inFlight || blocked} onClick={() => void submit()}>Send</button>
    {send.last?.state === "failed" && submitted && <p role="alert">Not sent: {submitted} <button onClick={() => setDraft(current => current ? current + " " + submitted : submitted)}>Edit</button> <button onClick={() => setSubmitted("")}>Dismiss</button></p>}</>;
}
```
Membership: no horizon, the conversation's own group and messages' audience prove presence/absence unless a pending prediction blocks absence. `loading` means acquisition or later withdrawal. Never add `with own keys` to admin-written memberships.
`denied` always blocks. `existence(member) === "absent"` blocks only when fresh and non-revalidating. Stale absence queues; `loading`/`partial` never disable.

`waitSeconds: 0` returns at custody; `inFlight` covers admission, ungated by acquisition after session readiness. Identity I/O: 15s excluding prior admissions; `usePendingWrites()` says “not yet admitted”. Reload loses it. Resumes FIFO; `E_ADMISSION_STORE`/`E_ADMISSION_TIMEOUT` permit retry after repair without remounting.
Predictions: “Sending…”.
Persist `{id: mintId(), args}` per account/backend before `run(args, { id, waitSeconds: 0 })`. `usePendingWrite`: “Saving…” during submission, “Checking…” while `restoring`; afterward undefined means unknown. Never auto-resend. Dismiss after lookup. New attempts: new ids; replay omits historical results. Web/Expo starters journal this.

`usePendingItems({writes,rows,scope,rowKey,intentKey})`: mapped row/intent union through observed settlement. `usePendingState(op,{scope,current,read?,value})`: latest admitted intent. [Reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md); non-JS hosts: “The device reply”.

## Read boundaries
`query(op, args)` settles ready/denied within 30 seconds including queues/approval. Admitted dependent writes: observed/failed or in complete predicted device answers. Unrelated writes pass; unknown intersects. Custody awaits events; loading/races before/after waits: four rounds; exhaustion refuses retryable `E_QUERY_RETRY`; proven permanently unavailable held reads refuse non-retryable `E_QUERY_HELD`. Offline: permitted predictions/data or immediate `E_OFFLINE`, including blockers. Refusals win; partial/stale permitted.

## Password React and Expo

Beside the persona form (`guide personas`), use this for `use identity password`:
```tsx
import { useState } from "react";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { AppProvider, useAuth } from "snapback4/react";
const app = createApp({ url: "http://127.0.0.1:4400", sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() });
export function PasswordApp() { return <AppProvider app={app}><Password /></AppProvider>; }
function Password() {
  const auth = useAuth(), [email, setEmail] = useState(""), [password, setPassword] = useState(""), [why, setWhy] = useState("");
  if (auth.state === "restoring") return <>Restoring…</>;
  if (auth.state === "signed-in") return <>Signed in as {auth.viewer}</>;
  const submit = async (signup: boolean) => { const r = await (signup ? auth.signup(email, password) : auth.login(email, password)); if (!r.ok) setWhy(r.why.message); };
  return <><input aria-label="Email" value={email} onChange={e => setEmail(e.target.value)} /><input aria-label="Password" type="password" value={password} onChange={e => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
    <button onClick={() => void submit(false)}>Sign in</button><button onClick={() => void submit(true)}>Sign up</button>{why}</>;
}
```
SDK: `{ok:true, session}` / `{ok:false, why}`; wire: `{session}` / `{denied}`. Expo imports `Platform` and `* as SecureStore`:
`sessionStore: Platform.OS === "web" ? localStorageSessionStore() : { get: SecureStore.getItemAsync, set: SecureStore.setItemAsync, delete: SecureStore.deleteItemAsync }`.
Synced web: `webDevice()`; native: `nativeDevice`; no-sync web: journal. `init --web`/`--expo` writes the form. REL owns the deploy checklist.

## React hooks
`matchRead(read, …)` (no type arguments; or a page) requires five states: `present(data, read)` excludes null; `partial(data, read)` keeps T; `denied(why, read)`; `absent(read)`/`loading(read)`. `otherwise(read)` covers omitted loading/absent/present; denied/partial stay explicit, or `...otherwiseAll(read => …)` anywhere takes all unnamed (named handlers win). `existence(read)` classifies; `isReady(read)` enables `read.existence`; `void client.close()` in cleanup.
Types/hooks: `RowOf`/`ResultOf`/`ArgsOf`/`UseQuery`/`UsePage`/`useSignedInViewer()`/`usePersonaApp`; `{ skip: !id }` waits for arguments; `[add, remove].find(isFailed)?.last.why`; helpers take `Outcome | undefined` or `Read<unknown>` ([form](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#status-components-and-a-form)).

| Meaning | UI decision |
| --- | --- |
| `loading` | Withheld: keep composers/headers/routes mounted. |
| `denied` | Show refusal; disable durable action. |
| `present` | Render values; `incomplete` controls “Partial results”. |
| `absent` | Proven empty: resolve missing route/row. |
| `partial` | Uncertain empty, never absence or denial; `incomplete` controls “Partial results”. |

`null`/`[]` is empty; `false`/`0`/nested nulls are present. Empty is `absent` only with `complete`, `cut === null`, no `next`, and no admission or intersecting prediction. Unknown prediction footprints block every read; complete local execution narrows dependencies. `fresh` never demotes; offline `absent` is absence at the acquired watermark. Hidden/missing rows are indistinguishable.
Route on `present`/`absent`; follow `useAuth().state` (personas: `guide personas`). Reset drafts on account/backend change. Retention grants no authority.
Deletes, policy changes and unknown/older evidence start a nonrenewable one-second bound; inserts, non-policy updates and plain revalidation retain through failures. Local bounds exclude queueing; online bounds include it. Bounded failure/timeout withdraws. Scope loss withdraws dependent groups; older replicas await publication. `keep: true`: 30-second subscriber-free linger; `keep: false`: immediate withdrawal. Keys, deadlines, sign-out, credentials/store/schema/generation changes withdraw immediately.
Search: render `page.items`; `readPresentation(page.read)` supplies `pending` (loading), `incomplete` (partial/coverage/cut/continuation), `stale`, `revalidating`, `why?`, `since?`; never permission. Follow `hasMore`/`loadMore`. Unknown counts never hide conversations. [Expo screen](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#a-stable-conversation-screen): backend/account/resource shell preserves drafts.

`app.online`/`useOnline()` (AppProvider): server query/observe, ephemeral mutate, act; no bearer/lifecycle; durable writes: app.mutate. `guide social`/`guide ephemeral`: snapshots/typing/quotes/bounded screens.

## Generic read props and outcomes

An empty complete list is `absent`. Generic list props: `Read<T[]>` (`T extends { id: string }`); operation props: `UseQuery<typeof api.op>`.
Narrow `write.state === "sent"` before `result` (omitted on replay); failed carries `why`; pending is not success.
`useQuery` returns a live Read, without `refetch()`. Relevant changes re-evaluate it. For a one-shot refetch, `await client.query(...)` after `sent`.

## Row queries
Share/paginate (`usePage` queries take `c: cursor ?` with `after c`); authorization remains dependent.
Eight active; eight queued observation attempts start/turn; direct queries and timed presence refreshes use separate paths. Local budgets pause while queued, never replenish. Online evidence overtakes ≤8 times; occupied slots can withdraw.

Server/composed scopes preserve unrelated rooms; unknown selectors withdraw table-wide.
Flashes: `inspect(client)`, `useInspection(import.meta.env.DEV)`: [pressure](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#inspect-subscription-pressure).

## Expo and acquisition
Browser: `createApp`; no sync → journal (personas too). Synced: `webDevice()`/writes: "online". Opening refusal preserves replay. Native: `nativeDevice`; Expo Go online; `guide sync`.
Every milestone: `npm run export:ios`; proof in the background: `witness status --wait`. Rebuild: `npm run native:smoke`. Acquisition/offline reopen/reconnect/write replay: `guide witness`.

`readOnly: true` (`createClient`/online `createApp`, `guide read-only`): inactive `useSeen`, no SDK rotation. `refreshSession()`: `E_READ_ONLY_REFRESH`; `expiresAt`: `E_AUTH`.

## Receipts
`guide receipts`: `useSeen(api.receiptFamilies.chat, { scope, through, active })` owns retries. SDK owns delivery reporting; screen owns visible `seen` reporting. Supply visible confirmed positions; separate retained history/live decorations.

fn evaluate_leaf(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow => Ok(true),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Ok(false),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? == term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? != term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Compare(op,left,right) => {
            let left = term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let right = term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let (Value::Int(a),Value::Int(b)) = (left,right) else { return Ok(false); };
            Ok(match op { crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte => a >= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt => a > b,
                crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte => a <= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lt => a < b, _ => false })
        }
        RuleExpr::And(_) | RuleExpr::Or(_) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => unreachable!("compound rule"),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
            // Bind each operand once, through one codec/allocation path for both leaves.
            let created = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Created { .. });
            if created && !created_allowed {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Schema("created probes are valid only in insert rules".into()));
            }
            let key = key.iter().map(|term| self::term(term, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            if created {
                if !probe.created(table, index, &key) { return Ok(false); }
                if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
            }
            let equalities = equalities.iter().map(|(column, value)| Ok((column.clone(), term(value, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?))).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, crate::error::RuntimeError>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            probe.exists(table, index, &key, &equalities)
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn term(term: &RuleTerm, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    Ok(match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => { probe.field(field, false); next.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) => { probe.field(field, true); old.and_then(|old| old.field(field).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::Reference { column, old: previous, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, old: previous, .. } => {
            probe.field(column, *previous);
            let row = if *previous { old } else { Some(next) };
            let mut value = row.and_then(|r| r.field(column).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null);
            for (table, field) in term.references() {
                match probe.reference(table, &value, field)? {
                    Some(next) => value = next,
                    None => { *missing = true; return Ok(Value::Null); }
                }
            }
            value
        },
        RuleTerm::Viewer => Value::Id(viewer.to_owned()),
        RuleTerm::Literal(value) => value.clone(),
    })
}

pub(crate) fn rule_probes(rule: &RuleExpr) -> u64 {

    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => hops(&[a,b]),

//! The default `init --web` and `init --expo` starters: one table, one live
//! query, three mutations and one screen. Toolchain pins come from the
//! maintained reference apps, so the starters move with them; the reference
//! chat apps themselves stay reachable as `--template chat`.
use serde_json::{Value, json};

const SCHEMA: &str = include_str!("starter/schema.q");
/// Rows and cases for the starter's own `todos` schema; init writes them only
/// beside that schema (see `init`), never beside a project's own.
pub(super) const FIXTURES: [(&str, &str); 2] = [
    ("fixtures/todos.jsonl", include_str!("starter/fixtures/todos.jsonl")),
    ("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl", include_str!("starter/fixtures/test.cases.jsonl")),
];

/// What init knows about the project before it writes the starter.
pub(super) struct Shape {
    /// The page and app name, from the directory.
    pub title: String,
    pub password: bool,
    /// Personas of a kept schema that declares `use personas` and no
    /// `use identity`: nobody can sign in, so the screen picks a persona.
    pub personas: Option<Vec<String>>,
    /// The screen behind the sign-in: the todo list over the starter's own
    /// operations, or the neutral `Home` beside a schema without them.
    pub body: Body,
    /// What the kept schema syncs, for the README's offline sentence.
    pub offline: Offline,
}

/// Which tables of the project's schema a device holds offline: the README
/// and init's summary say what works offline from this, never from the
/// starter's own (synced) todos schema.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.6 — wave 7 (R3): "works offline" was printed beside online-only schemas.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub(super) enum Offline {
    /// The schema does not compile (yet), or there is none: no claim either way.
    Unknown,
    /// The tables that declare `sync`, and how many tables there are (views excluded).
    Tables { synced: Vec<String>, total: usize },
}

impl Offline {
    /// The README paragraph (it always starts with "Offline", which is how
    /// `init --adapt` finds it again), wrapped like the rest of the README.
    pub(super) fn paragraph(&self, platform: Platform) -> String {
        let lead = match platform { Platform::Web => "Offline:", Platform::Expo => "Offline, in device builds and on the web:" };
        let body = match self {
            Offline::Unknown => "tables that declare `sync` read and write offline and sync when the link returns; the others read from the server, and writes to them made offline are kept on this device and sent when the link returns (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).".to_owned(),
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty() => format!("no table in `snapback/*.q` declares `sync`, so reads need the server. Writes made offline are kept {} and sent when the link returns. Add `sync` to a table to read it offline (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).", if platform == Platform::Web { "in this browser's principal journal" } else { "on this device" }),
            Offline::Tables { synced, total } if synced.len() >= *total => "every table declares `sync`, so the app reads and writes offline and syncs when the link returns.".to_owned(),
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } => format!("the synced {} ({}) {} and {} offline and {} when the link returns; the other tables read from the server, and writes to them made offline are sent when the link returns (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).",
                if synced.len() == 1 { "table" } else { "tables" }, synced.iter().map(|name| format!("`{name}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "),
                if synced.len() == 1 { "reads" } else { "read" }, if synced.len() == 1 { "writes" } else { "write" }, if synced.len() == 1 { "syncs" } else { "sync" }),
        };
        wrap(&format!("{lead} {body}"), 80)
    }

    /// init's one-line summary of the same fact, or nothing when unknown.
    pub(super) fn summary(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Offline::Unknown => "",
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty() => ", online reads (no table declares `sync`; offline writes queue in the write journal)",
            Offline::Tables { .. } => ", offline-capable for its synced tables",
        }
    }
}

/// Wrap at `width`, never inside a `code span`.
fn wrap(text: &str, width: usize) -> String {
    let mut words: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for word in text.split(' ') {
        match words.last_mut() {
            Some(open) if open.matches('`').count() % 2 == 1 => { open.push(' '); open.push_str(word); }
            _ => words.push(word.to_owned()),
        }
    }
    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for word in &words {
        match lines.last_mut() {
            Some(line) if line.len() + 1 + word.len() <= width => { line.push(' '); line.push_str(word); }
            _ => lines.push(word.clone()),
        }
    }
    lines.join("\n")
}

/// A README with its offline paragraph replaced by `OFFLINE`, and 0.4.22's
/// claim in the opening paragraph rewritten to today's opening, so `init
/// --adapt` recognizes an unmodified starter README whatever its schema
/// synced when it was written.

fn validate_arguments(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, kind) in &program.args {
        if !inputs.contains_key(name) && (kind.is_optional() || matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor)) {
            inputs.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
        }
        let value = inputs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input(format!("missing argument {name}")))?;
        validate_arg(schema, kind, value, name)?;
    }
    if let Some(extra) = inputs.keys().find(|name| !program.args.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{} takes no argument {extra}", program.name)));
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_arg(schema: &Schema, kind: &ArgType, value: &Value, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !kind.bounds_valid() {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} has a minimum greater than its maximum")));
    }
    crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
    if contains_system_principal(value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
    }
    match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor => match value {
            Value::Null => Ok(()),
            Value::String(token) => crate::cursor::decode_cursor(schema, token).map(|_| ()).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label}: {error}"))),
            _ => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be an opaque cursor string or null"))),
        },
        ArgType::Optional(_) if *value == Value::Null => Ok(()),
        ArgType::Optional(inner) => validate_arg(schema, inner, value, label),
        ArgType::Scalar => match value {
            Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be a scalar"))),
            _ => Ok(()),
        },
        ArgType::Text { max, min, format } => match value {
            Value::String(text) => {
                if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_text_control(text) {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
                }

//! The durable at-most-once record is the replay body. Replays retrieve
//! execution identity, never historical read authority (including scalar results).
use super::{MutationOutcome, WriteState};
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
use crate::error::{Refusal, RuntimeError};
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
use crate::meter::Metered;
use crate::value::Value;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};

impl MutationOutcome {
    pub fn reply(&self, id: Option<&str>) -> Json {
        let mut body = match &self.state {
            WriteState::Sent { seq, result } => {
                let mut body = json!({ "state": "sent", "id": id, "seq": seq });
                if !self.replayed { body["result"] = crate::wire::to_json(result); }
                body
            }
            WriteState::Failed { why, current } => {
                let mut body = json!({ "state": "failed", "id": id, "why": why });
                if !self.replayed && let Some(value) = current.as_ref().filter(|value| **value != Value::Null) { body["current"] = crate::wire::to_json(value); }
                body
            }
        };
        if self.replayed { body["replayed"] = Json::Bool(true); }
        body
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — store the replay body: no historical values, even in diagnostic messages or named-constraint detail.
    pub fn receipt(&self, id: Option<&str>) -> Json {
        let mut body = self.reply(id);
        body.as_object_mut().expect("reply object").remove("result");
        body.as_object_mut().expect("reply object").remove("current");
        if let WriteState::Failed { why, .. } = &self.state {
            body["why"]["message"] = Json::String(format!("the program refused with {}", why.code));
            body["why"].as_object_mut().expect("refusal object").remove("detail");
        }
        if self.receipt_only { body["receiptOnly"] = Json::Bool(true); }
        body["replayed"] = Json::Bool(true);
        body
    }

    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(super) fn from_receipt(text: &str) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let body: Json = serde_json::from_str(text).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        let malformed = || RuntimeError::Store("invalid mutation receipt".into());
        let state = match body["state"].as_str() {
            Some("sent") => WriteState::Sent { seq: body["seq"].as_u64().ok_or_else(malformed)?, result: Value::Null },
            Some("failed") => {
                let mut why: Refusal = serde_json::from_value(body["why"].clone()).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — even a historical W-shaped receipt cannot return row-derived diagnostic text.
                why.message = format!("the program refused with {}", why.code);
                why.detail = None;
                WriteState::Failed { why, current: None }
            }
            _ => return Err(malformed()),
        };
        Ok(Self { receipt_only: body.get("receiptOnly").and_then(Json::as_bool).unwrap_or(false), state, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: true })
    }
}
import { useState, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { AppProvider, useAuth, usePersonaApp } from "snapback4/react";
import { openApp, personas } from "./client.ts";

// `?as=bob` acts as the declared persona dev:bob; the picker switches it.
export function App() {
  const [persona, setPersona] = useState(() => new URLSearchParams(location.search).get("as"));
  // One app and write store per persona: opened on choice, closed on switch and unmount.
  const app = usePersonaApp(persona, openApp);
  const choose = (next: string | null) => {
    const address = new URL(location.href);
    if (next) address.searchParams.set("as", next); else address.searchParams.delete("as");
    history.replaceState(null, "", address);
    setPersona(next);
  };
  if (!persona) return <main>
    <h1>Act as</h1>
    {personas.map(name => <button key={name} onClick={() => choose(name)}>{name}</button>)}
  </main>;
  if (!app) return <p>Opening…</p>;
  return <AppProvider app={app}><Account leave={() => choose(null)}><Todos /></Account></AppProvider>;
}

// Every read and write runs as the persona; there is no session to restore.
function Account({ leave, children }: { leave: () => void; children: ReactNode }) {
  const auth = useAuth();
  if (auth.state === "signed-in") return children;
  return <main>
    <p>{auth.state === "restoring" ? "Opening…" : auth.why?.message ?? "Signed out"}</p>
    <button onClick={leave}>Choose a persona</button>
  </main>;
}
    head.replace(&format!("<Account leave={{{leave}}}><Todos /></Account>"), &format!("<Account leave={{{leave}}}><Home leave={{{leave}}} /></Account>"))
}

fn persona_home(home: &str) -> String {
    let replacements = [
        ("function Home() {", "function Home({ leave }: { leave: () => void }) {"),
        ("  const auth = useAuth(), link = useLink();", "  const link = useLink();"),
        (r#"<Button title="Sign out" onPress={() => void auth.logout()} />"#, r#"<Button title="Switch persona" onPress={leave} />"#),
        (r#"<button onClick={() => void auth.logout()}>Sign out</button>"#, r#"<button onClick={leave}>Switch persona</button>"#),
        ("// Never null here: Account renders this screen only once signed in.", "// Never null here: Account renders this screen only once the persona's app is open."),
    ];
    let mut home = home.to_owned();
    for (from, to) in replacements { home = home.replace(from, to); }
    assert!(!home.contains("auth.") && home.contains("Switch persona"), "the persona Home has no session to sign out of");
    home.replace("import { useAuth, useLink, useSignedInViewer }", "import { useLink, useSignedInViewer }")
}

/// One module from a sign-in head and a screen body, each written as a whole
/// file: their single-line named imports merge per module (packages before
/// `../`, then `./`), then the head's code, then the body's.
fn compose(head: &str, body: &str) -> String {
    fn split(text: &str) -> (Vec<&str>, &str) {
        let mut imports = Vec::new();
        let mut rest = text;
        while let Some(line) = rest.split_inclusive('\n').next().filter(|line| line.starts_with("import ")) {
            imports.push(line.trim_end());
            rest = &rest[line.len()..];
        }
        (imports, rest.trim_start_matches('\n'))
    }
    let (head_imports, head_code) = split(head);
    let (body_imports, body_code) = split(body);
    // (`import {` or `import type {`, module, names) in first-seen order.
    let mut merged: Vec<(String, String, Vec<String>)> = Vec::new();
    for line in head_imports.into_iter().chain(body_imports) {
        let (Some(open), Some(close)) = (line.find('{'), line.find('}')) else { panic!("starter imports are `import {{ … }} from \"…\";`: {line}") };
        let (kind, module) = (line[..open].trim().to_owned(), line[close + 1..].trim().to_owned());
        let names = line[open + 1..close].split(',').map(|name| name.trim().to_owned()).filter(|name| !name.is_empty());
        match merged.iter_mut().find(|(k, m, _)| *k == kind && *m == module) {
            Some((_, _, existing)) => for name in names { if !existing.contains(&name) { existing.push(name); } },
            None => merged.push((kind, module, names.collect())),
        }
    }
    merged.sort_by_key(|(_, module, _)| if module.contains("\"../") { 1 } else if module.contains("\"./") { 2 } else { 0 });
    let imports = merged.iter().map(|(kind, module, names)| format!("{kind} {{ {} }} {module}\n", names.join(", "))).collect::<String>();
    format!("{imports}\n{head_code}\n{body_code}")
}

const GUEST_SIGN_IN: &str = "Choose \"Continue as a guest\" to sign in (`use identity`); `npx snapback4 guide personas`\nadds password accounts and development personas.";
const PERSONA_SIGN_IN: &str = "The first screen (on the web also `?as=<name>`) acts as a persona your schema declares with\n`use personas`: `usePersonaApp` opens one app per persona and closes the old one on a switch.\nPersonas act only on `snapback4 dev`: add `use identity` and a sign-in screen before you\ndeploy (`npx snapback4 guide personas`).";

/// The starter README for a screen: the Todos README describes the starter's
/// own schema and screen; beside a project's schema (the neutral `Home`) the
/// README is named after the directory and describes that schema's sign-in.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (042): an adapted starter kept a README about todos and guest sign-in.
pub(super) fn readme(platform: Platform, title: &str, body: &Body, personas: bool, offline: &Offline) -> String {
    let todos = match platform { Platform::Web => include_str!("starter/web/README.md"), Platform::Expo => include_str!("starter/expo/README.md") };
    if *body == Body::Todos { return todos.to_owned(); }
    let home = match platform { Platform::Web => include_str!("starter/web/README.home.md"), Platform::Expo => include_str!("starter/expo/README.home.md") };
    let text = home.replacen("# TITLE", &format!("# {title}"), 1).replace("SIGN_IN", if personas { PERSONA_SIGN_IN } else { GUEST_SIGN_IN })
        .replacen("OFFLINE\n", &format!("{}\n", offline.paragraph(platform)), 1);
    if platform == Platform::Web { return text; }
    // The Expo toolchain notes are the Todos README's own, word for word.
    let advisories = todos.split_once("\n> ").map(|(_, rest)| format!("> {}", rest.split_once("\n\n").expect("the advisory box ends").0)).expect("the Expo README has its advisory box");
    let toolchain = todos.split_once("\nKeep the Expo and").map(|(_, rest)| format!("Keep the Expo and{}", rest.split_once("## Next steps").expect("the Expo README has next steps").0)).expect("the Expo README has its toolchain notes");
    text.replacen("ADVISORIES", &advisories, 1).replacen("TOOLCHAIN\n", &toolchain, 1)
}

/// The Expo app's `app.json` ids, named after the directory's title: a slug
/// (`bake-sale`), a URL scheme and a bundle id that start with a letter
/// (`bakesale`, `dev.snapback4.bakesale`).
pub(super) fn app_ids(title: &str) -> (String, String, String) {
    let words: Vec<String> = title.split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric()).filter(|word| !word.is_empty()).map(str::to_ascii_lowercase).collect();
    let slug = if words.is_empty() { "app".to_owned() } else { words.join("-") };
    let mut scheme = words.concat();
    if !scheme.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_alphabetic()) { scheme = format!("app{scheme}"); }

## 2. The form

```quarry
table issues:
  org: orgs
  title: text
  immutable org
  read <- exists members[.org, viewer]
  insert <- exists members[.org, viewer]

table comments:
  issue: issues
  author: principal
  body: text
  read through .issue
  insert <- .author = viewer
  delete <- .author = viewer
```

1. **`read through .ref` is the whole read rule.**
   - `.ref` is one required, immutable reference column naming the parent by id, with no default.
   - The parent is an **ordinary application table**. A maintained view's generated table, a materialized view, and any other engine-owned table are refused as parents in this revision: their rows appear and disappear by derivation, outside the lifetime rules of §3.
   - No other read rule may be declared. A narrower child (a private comment only its author reads) keeps today's hand-copied form. Narrowing conjuncts are not in this revision (§9).
   - A service grant belongs on the parent, and the child inherits it with the rest of the parent's rule. A service disjunct on the child alone (`read through .issue or viewer = 'service:search'`) is refused in this revision: the parent's verdict could not decide it.
2. **The meaning.** The child is readable by exactly the viewers who can read its parent now. Given §3, that is the same as the parent's rule evaluated over the inputs the child captured at insert (§4).
3. **Writes inherit it.** An insert of a child requires two things of its parent: it is **live** (present and not expired), and its read rule **admits the writer**. That covers a plain insert, an upsert's insert arm, and `insert … if absent`. The requirement is implicit and stated on the rules card; the child's `insert` rule adds conditions to it. Updates and deletes do not read the parent, because the reference is immutable and the capture never changes.
4. **Chains.** A parent that itself declares `read through` passes on its own captured inputs: the child copies the parent's stored captures, never walking to a live grandparent. A cycle is refused.

`exists t[.ref]` and `created t[.ref]` keep their meanings.

## 3. A child lives within its parent

Three rules make every live child's captured inputs equal to its live parent's fields:

1. **No delete under a live child.** Deleting a row of a table that some `read through` references requires two things:
   - **read authority on that row, for every delete attempt, with children or without.** A caller who may delete but not read gets the ordinary hidden-target refusal, so the refusal never tells it whether hidden children exist;
   - no **live** child row referencing it.

   The refusals are in §6. The application deletes the children first (a bounded loop or a self-chunking job), then the parent. Cascading deletion is a referential action, not in this revision (§9). The live-child probe runs in the deleting transaction, after the delete rule, so jobs and seed deletes (the system principal) cannot skip it. Children inserted earlier in the same transaction count.
2. **A child expires no later than its parent.** Every row of a `read through` table has an **effective deadline**, defined recursively:

   `deadline(row) = min(authored(row), captured parent deadline)`

   Here `authored` is the row's own `expire at` column, if the table declares one, and a null or absent value means never. The child captures its parent's **effective** deadline, which already includes the parent's own chain, so a middle table with no `expire at` of its own still passes its parent's deadline on.
   - **The captured parent deadline is its own immutable field**, stamped at insert and never written again.
   - The child's authored `expire at` column may stay mutable, as today. The effective deadline is **recomputed** as `min(authored, captured parent deadline)` on every write that touches the row: insert, update, upsert, import, activation, and a **trusted restore**. An instant stored in a restored image is never trusted. No write can raise it above the captured parent deadline. Setting the authored column earlier kills the child earlier; setting it later never outlives the parent.
   - The effective deadline and the captured parent deadline are **engine columns**, like the captures. An activation projection keeps them.
   - A middle parent's authored expiry is immutable once any child captures its effective deadline (§4.2), so a middle parent can never shorten under its descendants.
   - The effective deadline is **stored with the row and indexed**. There is **one expiring classification**: a table is expiring when it declares `expire at` or when its rows can carry a finite captured deadline. Every site that today asks whether `expire at` is declared asks this instead, and the list below is illustrative, not closed. That includes `trust_synced_read`, mutation page admission and the hidden-maintain reservation. Every gate reads the stored instant:
     - point and live lookups (`lookup_live`);
     - rule probes and unique checks;
     - snapshots and the sweep, which gains an expiry index for the table;
     - commit-end retirement;
     - live aggregates and cap counts;
     - subscription `next_expiry`;
     - partition grant lifetimes;
     - the classification of a source as expiring.
   - A captured parent deadline must be immutable on the parent. That holds by construction for a `read through` parent, and requires `immutable` on an authored parent column.
3. **No reuse and no removal under a physical child.** A parent id cannot be inserted again, even by replacing an expired row with the same id, while any **physical** child row references it. That covers every stored child, live or expired, swept or not. The insert refuses exactly as a collision with a live id does. The sweep never removes a parent row while a physical child references it. Children's deadlines are no later than their parents', so the sweep takes child tables before parent tables and removes children first.

**What follows.**
- A parent that is gone, whether deleted or expired, has no live children.
- On the server, an id is reused only after every physical child that referenced it has been removed. A new parent cannot meet an old child there: not in a read, an aggregate or activation. A **device** can still hold an old child briefly, because a parent and its children travel in different partition logs, or for longer under `retain delivered-history`. §5 makes the device's verdict safe against that.
- Because live children never belong to a dead parent, no incarnation is needed.
- Captured inputs are immutable on the parent (§4), so a live child's captures equal its live parent's fields.

## 4. The capture

A table that declares `read through .ref` gets hidden, engine-owned fields. They are a **sibling** of recorded fields: they share the payload refusal, the wire and query split, and the device coverage check, but not the public-target path. Unlike recorded fields, these fields are required, hold private parent fields, are refused before stamping, and are recaptured under §7. They are:
- stamped at insert;
- stored with the row image;
- stripped from query rows, `*` shapes and generated types;
- refused in any payload (`E_RECORDED_FIELD`).

1. **What is captured.**
   - Every parent field that the parent's read rule reads, with every conjunct included. Probes stay live: `exists members[.org, viewer]` becomes `exists members[.$issue.org, viewer]`, evaluated against the current `members`.
   - The parent's **effective deadline** (§3.2), or never.
   - The parent's own captures, in a chain.
2. **Captured parent fields must be immutable** on the parent. That covers every column its read rule reads and any authored expiry column. Each missing `immutable` is a refusal whose rewrite is the line to add. A captured value keeps its type, nullable or not: "total" (next item) means the field is always stamped, not that its value cannot be null.
3. **Captures are total.** A committed child always has every capture, because an absent or expired parent refuses the insert before anything is stamped (§6). `audience_binding` therefore treats a captured prefix as a required group key, like an authored required column.
4. **Order in the writing transaction**, the same on every insert path and on server and device:
   1. refuse client values for hidden fields;
   2. reserve the capture's fixed charge: one parent point read, the parent rule's static probe allowance, and a heap allowance bounded by the parent's declared field sizes. It depends on nothing about the parent. For an upsert and for `insert … if absent`, the reservation is made **once, before** the engine chooses between the update arm, a hidden collision and the insert arm, and it is consumed without being charged twice. Both worlds then return the same code with the same restored meter, whether the ceiling can hold the reservation or not;
   3. resolve the parent **live**: same transaction, not expired, including a parent inserted earlier in the same mutation;
   4. if it is absent or expired, refuse (§6), and never evaluate the rule over nulls;
   5. stamp the captures;
   6. evaluate the parent's rule over them for the writer, then the child's `insert` rule.
5. **Maintain paths through the reference reuse the capture.** A maintain path that names a parent field the child captures, such as `view unread: count receipts by .message.chat, .principal` where `receipts` declares `read through .message`, reads that inherited capture.
   - It is **not** a historical recorded path, and it is allowed although the parent is private, because the capture already exists on the row. Ordinary historical recorded paths keep their existing public-target contract.
   - It takes the capture's type and activation lifecycle.
   - For rule derivation, it counts as that one captured rule field and no other. The view's column is named for the parent field (`chat`). The derived read rule is the parent's rule rewritten onto that column: `exists members[.chat, viewer]`.
   - A rule field that the group key does not cover still refuses with `E_MAINTAIN_READ_RULE`.
   - Sync is inherited only when that column is the audience key, exactly as for an authored group key.
6. **The dependency identity** is:
   - the parent's read rule, expanded;
   - the captured fields' types, optionality and defaults, and the parent's expiry column;
   - the parent's own captures;
   - the child's reference binding: its target table, its type, and the refusal of a default.

   `adopt` compares it between generations. When it changes, activation recaptures (§7), and it recomputes every effective deadline. For this kind of capture only, recapture overrides the recorded path's "a stored value is never re-resolved". Public attribution paths keep that rule.

Developer evidence (Lane MP round 1, LLP 3000.015 §2.1) records the capture
branch that refused: invalid reference, missing/expired parent, or parent read
rule. Its source is the child's `read through` declaration. Caller E_RULE
serialization and the reserved charge are unchanged; expired and missing
parents intentionally share one developer clause. Round 2 also retains capture
as the actual authorization phase when its allowance exhausts: the source is
still `read through`, even though the caller's neutral E_RULE has insert effect.
The query-side collector belongs to the checked-out reader generation, and the
dev response-layer fallback prevents an uninstrumented refusal or empty read
from displaying an earlier request's cause (LLP 3000.015 §2.1).

# LLP 3000.005: Native jobs and reads — the app's Rust in the data plane, hosted as wasm, metered like a job, the same on the device

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Quarry (`job name(): native`); the engine's job runner and a wasm host (`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/native.rs`); the guest SDK (`crates/snapback4-native`); `check`, `dev`, `run`, `deploy`, `serve`, `doctor`; the guide; the generated Rust types
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1 (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-09-13
**Revised:** 2026-09-16 (review repairs: bounded native bridge, exact module continuation identity and Cargo artifact selection); 2026-09-13 (ticket repair qualification: logical device expiry and generation-bound native cache); 2026-09-13 (§9: the device-side twin — native reads, the author's ruling on decision 3; §6 revised; §8 the second landing); 2026-09-13 (§8: what landed — the fixture's numbers, the fuel measurement, the CLI proof; Status Implemented); 2026-09-13 (initial: the author's question, the four rules, the design, the fixture, what is not built)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (root; §7 carries the decision), [LLP 3000.004](./3000.004-feeds-and-the-interview-reference-app.rfc.md) (feeds: the serving half is the substrate's, the model half the app's — this document gives the model half a seat), [LLP 2000.000 §6](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) (the meter) and §10 (the effects host question), [LLP 1016 §4](./1016-administrative-plane.rfc.md) (jobs as sweeps, carried into Snapback 4), [`snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`](../snapback4/NOT-DOING.md) (the Moves line this document writes)

## Summary

The author asked, after LLP 3000.004: *what if we let you write Rust programs
that could access the database and generate a feed that the normal Snapback
live query could serve — would that be crazy?* Not crazy. It is the open
effects-host question of LLP 2000.000 §10 with a Rust answer, and it fits
Snapback 4 better than the JavaScript options because the pieces exist: the
interpreter runs over a `Rows` trait, the engine already runs jobs as the
system principal one chunk per commit under a meter, and the device core
already compiles to wasm.

This document specifies **native jobs**: a job whose body is not Quarry but
the app's own Rust, compiled to wasm by `snapback4 check`, hosted by the
engine with the same words as any job. Four rules keep it a job rather than a
hole in the design:

1. **Quarry still declares the feed.** The table, its rules, its horizon and
   its `sync` line are in `schema.q`; the Rust fills it. The one-language
   principle holds: effect bodies were always allowed in another language.
2. **Writes go through the engine's write path, never raw SQL**, so rules'
   companions — validation, constraints, maintains, partition events, the
   log — fire, and the ordinary live query serves the result for free.
3. **Reads go through the engine's read path, so the meter counts them.**
   A native job gets a job's words: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings,
   parked on refusal, listed by `doctor`, run by `snapback4 run`.
4. **Pure over the store.** The module imports one host function and
   nothing else: no clock, no randomness, no network. It runs in the
   simulator. Network-bound ranking stays outside and writes into tables
   the native job then reads.

What it buys: floats, vectors, real loops and a typed schema for the scoring
half of a feed, none of which belong in Quarry; and, after the native module
landing, one Rust function over `Rows` that can run on the server as the job
and on the device as the personalizer. What it costs: `check` runs `cargo`
when `snapback/native/` exists (a warm no-op build is a few hundred
milliseconds), and the binary carries a wasm interpreter.

## 1. The question (author, 2026-09-13)

> What if we let you write Rust programs that could access the database and
> generate a feed that the normal Snapback live query could serve. Would
> that be crazy?

The lead answered with the four rules above and three hosting options, and
recommended wasm inside the stock binary. The author: "Write up an LLP about
this and then implement." This document is that LLP; §8 records what landed.

## 2. Where it sits

LLP 3000.004 §3 drew the line: candidate generation and learned scoring are
not in the data plane. That stays true of *Quarry*: no floats, no loops over
the corpus, no model. This document moves the line for *Rust*: a native job
computes over the store with the same access a Quarry job has, under the
same bounds, and no more. The external ranker of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 (a worker
with a service principal writing through mutations) remains the shape for
anything that needs the network; a native job is the shape for anything that
needs only the rows.

Hosting was the real decision. Three options were weighed:

- **Wasm inside the stock binary** — the app's Rust compiles to
  `wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the server hosts it with fuel metering and host
  functions that are exactly the engine's read and write paths; a release
  stays a stock binary plus one `.wasm` file. Sandboxed, portable, no
  per-app server build, no unsafe code in the core.
- **A cdylib over the existing JSON C ABI** — simpler, no wasm runtime; but
  a crash takes the server down and the ABI is fragile across toolchains.
- **The app owns the server binary, Exact2-style** — most natural for
  Interview; but it ends `npx snapback4` and the loop bar's fresh-agent
  assumption.

**Decision (lead): wasm, with `wasmi`.** `wasmi` is a small, pure-Rust
interpreter with fuel metering, already in the dependency cache; `wasmtime`
is the upgrade if throughput ever demands it. A native job is optional: an
app without `snapback/native/` never pays for it, and the loop bar and the
bakeoff are untouched.

## 3. The authoring surface

```q
# LLP 3000.007: Relaxed Quarry and explicit residency — re-deriving the language's constraints under the meter

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Accepted — the author has ruled every question put to it (§14, rulings 1–33, 2026-09-13 through 2026-09-17; the 2026-09-17 delegation is recorded there and its decisions in §21.8). The original A0–E sequence of §12 landed first; implementation since has run as waves 1–15, recorded in §17–§21 and in `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sections that are not accepted say so in their own text.

## Summary

This RFC is cross-referenced by
[LLP 3000.008](./3000.008-holistic-product-excellence.rfc.md). Its
[Quarry child](./3000.008.000-ordinary-quarry-programming.rfc.md) adds
product-level acceptance cases, while the residency and client children cover
the corresponding outcomes. LLP 3000.008 does not alter this RFC's accepted
rulings or implementation record.

The author, after building Clique on Snapback: *Quarry seems great except
too constraining. Its biggest value is describing data that is synced
locally and in the cloud and queried with one language. If you don't need
that, something like Convex or Supabase — more flexible, even if you lose
some nice properties — is probably easier for agents.* He asked for the
thesis to be analysed and, if it holds, for a more relaxed way to use
Snapback 4 where local data is not needed, or relaxed across other seams,
that makes it ergonomic for agents and lets the Clique benchmark run
smoothly. Then, seeing the residency draft he had already commissioned
(LLP 3000.006), he asked whether to push further.

The thesis is half right, and the half that is right points somewhere
more specific than "relax when offline is not needed."

**Right:** the constraints that cost builders time are constraints on the
*program* — a local binds once, no accumulators, no `continue`, no `else
if`, no `where`, no text `+`, no `/` — and every one of them is an
inheritance from Snapback 2's static byte-and-step pricing, not a
requirement of running on a device. LLP 2000.000 §4.1 said so when it
flipped the bound model: *"Decide the bound model, keep the language, and
re-derive its restrictions."* Snapback 4 copied the binder and never
re-derived them. The evidence that they are not device constraints is in
the code: the IR carries `Assign`, `Push`, `Continue`, `Concat` and a
scan `range`; the interpreter — one implementation on the server, in the
wasm and in the native module — runs them today; only the binder refuses
them.

**Not right:** the value that survives without local sync is larger than
"nice properties." Declarative rules that are the audience, typed
refusals with rewrites, the runtime meter, identity, typed assets with a
lifecycle, expiry, jobs on the server's clock and a process that embeds
no JavaScript are why the same builder was faster than Convex on five of
seven Clique milestones in the 0.2.6 run and why authorization is the
dimension Snapback wins. Those do not depend on `sync`. And where the
weaker builders stopped, the cause was not the program language at all:
it was two *model* frictions — spelling the audience twice (the read rule
and the `sync` line) and counters written by one principal into rows
another principal owns — which the mechanism can remove.

So the proposal is not a second, looser language for online-only apps.
It is: **one language, relaxed once for every host under the meter that
already bounds it; the sync audience inferred from the read rule; write
authority decided by the write rule; residency and prediction made
legible instead of inferred at runtime; the online-only mode paved; the
client and dev seams the lanes hit closed; two escape hatches specified
for the last ten percent; and a bar that measures exactly the ceremony
this document claims to remove.** §10 maps every correction loop the five
Clique lanes recorded to the item that removes it. A review received on
rev 1 corrected four claims; rev 3 carries the corrections in §5.0, §5.3,
§5.6, §5.9 and §6.2 and the dispositions beside the review.

     1	//! Read-only production and fixture authoring notes; never an execution gate.
     2	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
     3	use snapback4_lang::{bind::Bound, Diagnostic, Site};
     4	use snapback4_core::{ir::{self, ProgramKind, ArgType}, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}, engine::validate, wire};
     5	use crate::project::Project;
     6	
     7	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — positive bootstrap dependencies form a least fixed point.
     8	pub(super) fn notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
     9	    let mut reachable = BTreeSet::new();
    10	    let mut system = BTreeSet::new();
    11	    let mut writers = BTreeSet::new();
    12	    for p in &bound.programs {
    13	        for (table, effect) in ir::write_tables(&p.program) {
    14	            if matches!(effect, "insert" | "upsert") {
    15	                writers.insert(table.clone());
    16	                if p.program.kind == ProgramKind::Job { system.insert(table); }
    17	            }
    18	        }
    19	        // Native jobs are opaque to the authoring analysis; no absence claim.
    20	        if p.program.native { return fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture); }
    21	    }
    22	    loop {
    23	        let previous = reachable.len();
    24	        for (name, table) in bound.schema.visible_tables() {
    25	            if bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some() { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); continue; }
    26	            if !writers.contains(name) { continue; }
    27	            let refs_ready = table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|through| reachable.contains(&through.parent));
    28	            if refs_ready && (system.contains(name) || possible(&table.rules.insert, &reachable)) { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); }
    29	        }
    30	        if reachable.len() == previous { break; }
    31	    }
    32	    let sources = project.sources().unwrap_or_default();
    33	    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    34	    for (name, table) in bound.schema.visible_tables() {
    35	        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some() || reachable.contains(name) { continue; }
    36	        let site = sources.iter().find_map(|(file, source)| source.lines().enumerate().find(|(_, line)| line.trim_start().starts_with(&format!("table {name}:"))).map(|(line, _)| Site::new(file, line as u32 + 1, 1)))
    37	            .unwrap_or_else(|| Site::new(&format!("table {name}"), 1, 1));
    38	        notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
    39	            format!("{name}: no production insert path can bootstrap this table; fixtures and /seed exist only in dev (an existing role/reference may be required)"),
    40	            "add an insert mutation with a declared service seat to bootstrap the first row; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin").guide("deploy"));
    41	    }
    42	    notes.extend(fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture));
    43	    notes
    44	}
    45	fn possible(rule: &RuleExpr, rows: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
    46	    match rule {
    47	        RuleExpr::Deny => false,
    48	        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
    49	        RuleExpr::Exists { table, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, .. } => rows.contains(table),
    50	        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|item| possible(item, rows)),
    51	        RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|item| possible(item, rows)),
    52	        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => {
    53	            if let (RuleTerm::Literal(a), RuleTerm::Literal(b)) = (left, right) { return a == b; }
    54	            let principal = match (left, right) { (RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(v)) | (RuleTerm::Literal(v), RuleTerm::Viewer) => v.as_str().ok(), _ => None };
    55	            if principal.is_some_and(|p| p.starts_with("service:")) { return true; }
    56	            if principal.is_some_and(|p| p.starts_with("dev:")) { return false; }
    57	            left.references().chain(right.references()).all(|(table, _)| rows.contains(table))
    58	        }
    59	        // Unknown predicates are potentially satisfiable: this lint is not a theorem of reachability.
    60	        RuleExpr::Ne(_, _) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, _, _) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => true,
    61	    }
    62	}
    63	fn fixture_notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    64	    let path = fixture.map(std::path::PathBuf::from).unwrap_or_else(|| project.root.join("fixtures")).join(super::test_cases::FILE);
    65	    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) else { return vec![]; };
    66	    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    67	    for (index, line) in text.lines().enumerate() {
    68	        let line = line.trim();
    69	        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { continue; }
    70	        let check = || -> Result<(), String> {
    71	            let case: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    72	            let op = case["op"].as_str().ok_or("case needs op")?;
    73	            let program = &bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op).ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown operation {op}"))?.program;
    74	            let empty = serde_json::json!({});
    75	            let args = case.get("args").unwrap_or(&empty).as_object().ok_or("args must be an object")?;
    76	            for (name, kind) in &program.args {
    77	                if !args.contains_key(name) && !kind.is_optional() && !matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor) { return Err(format!("{op}: missing argument {name}")); }
    78	            }
    79	            for (name, value) in args {
    80	                let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{op} takes no argument {name}"))?;
    81	                // Saved answers are runtime values; never claim a type failure for one.
    82	                if saved(value) { continue; }
    83	                let value = wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|e| format!("{name}: {e}"))?;
    84	                validate::validate_arg(&bound.schema, kind, &value, name).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    85	            }
    86	            Ok(())
    87	        };
    88	        if let Err(reason) = check() {
    89	            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&Site::new(&path.to_string_lossy(), index as u32 + 1, 1), "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT", reason,
    90	                "update this case's arguments to the operation's current signature; run snapback4 test").guide("testing"));
    91	        }
    92	    }
    93	    notes
    94	}
    95	fn saved(value: &serde_json::Value) -> bool {
    96	    match value { serde_json::Value::String(s) => s.starts_with('$') && s.len() > 1, serde_json::Value::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), serde_json::Value::Object(items) => items.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    97	}

//! Writes: the four words, their rules, the constraints (unique, cap,
//! immutable, append-only, after-insert), maintained aggregates, and the
//! partition events every row change produces.

use super::runner::Runner;
use super::validate::validate_row;
use crate::error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError};
use crate::partition;
use crate::schema::{Constraint, Effect, Table, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir};
use crate::rows::index_values;
use crate::value::{Value, Supplied, canonical_json};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum Origin {
    Source,
    Maintained,
}

#[path = "write_target.rs"]
pub(crate) mod target;
#[path = "write_references.rs"]
mod references;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, false).map(|_| ())
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_if_absent(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, true)
    }

    fn insert_mode(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && if_absent && self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("insert if absent on a read-through child queues without a row".into()));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            let mark = runner.meter.mark();
            let result = runner.insert_attempt(table_name, row, if_absent, None).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, Effect::Insert));
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — denied direct allocations hide deferred
            // read witnesses and counter work just as NOT_FOUND hides targets.
            if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Rule { effect: Effect::Insert, .. } | RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. }))
                && runner.schema.tables.get(table_name).is_some_and(|table| !table.sequences.is_empty()) {
                runner.meter.restore(mark);
            }
            result
        })
    }

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));

        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {

        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.

        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.

        // at the ceiling. Never use the clamped authority candidate as a key.

+## Password React and Expo
+
+Beside the persona form (`guide personas`), use this for `use identity password`:
+```tsx
+import { useState } from "react";
+import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
+import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
+import { AppProvider, useAuth } from "snapback4/react";
+const app = createApp({ url: "http://127.0.0.1:4400", sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() });
+export function PasswordApp() { return <AppProvider app={app}><Password /></AppProvider>; }
+function Password() {
+  const auth = useAuth(), [email, setEmail] = useState(""), [password, setPassword] = useState(""), [why, setWhy] = useState("");
+  if (auth.state === "restoring") return <>Restoring…</>;
+  if (auth.state === "signed-in") return <>Signed in as {auth.viewer}</>;
+  const submit = async (signup: boolean) => { const r = await (signup ? auth.signup(email, password) : auth.login(email, password)); if (!r.ok) setWhy(r.why.message); };
+  return <><input aria-label="Email" value={email} onChange={e => setEmail(e.target.value)} /><input aria-label="Password" type="password" value={password} onChange={e => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
+    <button onClick={() => void submit(false)}>Sign in</button><button onClick={() => void submit(true)}>Sign up</button>{why}</>;
+}
+```
+SDK: `{ok:true, session}` / `{ok:false, why}`; wire: `{session}` / `{denied}`. Expo imports `Platform` and `* as SecureStore`:
+`sessionStore: Platform.OS === "web" ? localStorageSessionStore() : { get: SecureStore.getItemAsync, set: SecureStore.setItemAsync, delete: SecureStore.deleteItemAsync }`.
+Synced web: `webDevice()`; native: `nativeDevice`; no-sync web: journal. `init --web`/`--expo` writes the form. REL owns the deploy checklist.
+
 ## React hooks
 `matchRead(read, …)` (no type arguments; or a page) requires five states: `present(data, read)` excludes null; `partial(data, read)` keeps T; `denied(why, read)`; `absent(read)`/`loading(read)`. `otherwise(read)` covers omitted loading/absent/present; denied/partial stay explicit, or `...otherwiseAll(read => …)` anywhere takes all unnamed (named handlers win). `existence(read)` classifies; `isReady(read)` enables `read.existence`; `void client.close()` in cleanup.
 Types/hooks: `RowOf`/`ResultOf`/`ArgsOf`/`UseQuery`/`UsePage`/`useSignedInViewer()`/`usePersonaApp`; `{ skip: !id }` waits for arguments; `[add, remove].find(isFailed)?.last.why`; helpers take `Outcome | undefined` or `Read<unknown>` ([form](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#status-components-and-a-form)).

+## Generic read props and outcomes
+
+An empty complete list is `absent`. Generic list props: `Read<T[]>` (`T extends { id: string }`); operation props: `UseQuery<typeof api.op>`.
+Narrow `write.state === "sent"` before `result` (omitted on replay); failed carries `why`; pending is not success.
+`useQuery` returns a live Read, without `refetch()`. Relevant changes re-evaluate it. For a one-shot refetch, `await client.query(...)` after `sent`.
+
 ## Row queries
 Share/paginate (`usePage` queries take `c: cursor ?` with `after c`); authorization remains dependent.
 Eight active; eight queued observation attempts start/turn; direct queries and timed presence refreshes use separate paths. Local budgets pause while queued, never replenish. Online evidence overtakes ≤8 times; occupied slots can withdraw.

 ## One-view photos
-Mount **above the list**; key: `JSON.stringify([backend, account, messageId, assetId])`. Open once; recycling/refetch preserves handles. Unmount/account change revokes late responses. Close on `absent`/denied/tombstone/changed-asset/deadline; deadlines run through loading.

    26	## Wire and client envelopes
    27	
    28	| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
    29	|---|---|---|
    30	| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
    31	| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}`; never treat it as an empty answer |
    32	| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
    33	| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
    34	| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |
    35	
    36	SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. HTTP status alone is insufficient.
    37	An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
    38	row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
    39	it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

    43	- `/q/<op>` with `{"args":{..}}` answers `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer}`

    46	  Page by passing `next` back as the query's cursor argument.

    48	  `{state:"failed", id, why, current?}`, both HTTP 200 (see Refusals for the
    49	  status codes). `result` is what the mutation returns (an inserted row's id is

    16

    18	For Expo, use **one command**: `bun add snapback4@latest snapback4-expo@latest`.
    19	The adapter requires the exact core version, including the patch; `check`
    20	warns on stderr if installed versions differ (also with --json). Rebuild your native development/release app
    21	after changing the adapter (Expo Go uses the online tier). Commit the lockfile,
    22	run `bunx snapback4 check`, typecheck the app and run your acceptance tests.
    23	`snapback4 test` uses ./fixtures when present; `--fixture dir` overrides it.
