# Todos: a Snapback 4 Expo starter

An Expo (React Native) app over a local-first Snapback 4 backend: one table,
one live query and three mutations. Development and production builds open the
native device and work offline; Expo Go uses the online client; web uses the
wasm device.

> **`npm install` takes a few minutes and reports about 15 high-severity
> advisories. They are expected; do not run `npm audit fix --force`** (it
> "fixes" them by downgrading Expo to SDK 44). All come from two packages in
> Expo's development toolchain with no patched release; neither is in the app
> bundle. Details under [Audit findings](#audit-findings).

```sh
npm install
npx snapback4 check      # compiles snapback/schema.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
npx snapback4 dev        # serves http://127.0.0.1:4400 and reloads on save
npm start                # in a second terminal: Expo Go, or press w for web
npm run typecheck
```

`npm run ios:dev` builds the native development build. The iOS simulator
reaches http://127.0.0.1:4400 on this machine (`init --expo --port N` writes
that port; `EXPO_PUBLIC_SNAPBACK_URL` overrides it); for a physical phone set
`EXPO_PUBLIC_SNAPBACK_URL` to your TLS deployment. Choose "Continue as a
guest", then add a few todos.

## Files

- `snapback/schema.q`: the backend (tables, rules, `sync`, queries, mutations).
  Edit it and `snapback4 dev` re-adopts it; `snapback4 check` regenerates the API.
- `fixtures/`: seed rows and expected outcomes for `snapback4 test`.
- `src/client.ts`: one `createApp` for the whole app; it picks the native
  device, the online client (Expo Go) or `webDevice()` (web).
- `App.tsx`: sign-in, the live list (`usePage`) and writes (`useMutation`).
  `usePage` passes the query's cursor itself: a paged query takes `c: cursor ?`
  and reads `after c` (`npx snapback4 guide reads`); any other query reads
  with `useQuery`. Props name hook results by operation (`UsePage<typeof
  api.list>`, `UseQuery<…>`, `UseMutation<…>`), and `useSignedInViewer()` is
  the viewer below the sign-in. After you replace the schema,
  `npx snapback4 init --adapt` swaps this screen for a `Home` that compiles
  against any schema, while it is unmodified.
- `expo-env.d.ts`: Expo's types, including `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_*`.
- `metro.config.js` and `metro-wasm-transformer.cjs`: keep both; Metro then
  ships the web device's wasm and skips the local `.snapback4/` store.

Keep the Expo and `babel-preset-expo` major versions together; mixing them can
fail Hermes compilation.

## Audit findings

`package.json`'s `overrides` moves the config plugins' `xcode` from the
deprecated `uuid@7` to `uuid@11` (GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq). `npm audit` still
reports about 15 high findings from two advisories in Expo's development
toolchain, with no patched release of either package in any version:
`braces` (GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm, a stack-exhaustion denial of service on deeply
nested glob patterns; Metro's file watcher through `micromatch`) and
`node-forge` (GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv, RSA signature verification; the Expo CLI's
code-signing for updates). Neither is part of the app bundle, and the starter
passes neither untrusted patterns nor signatures to them. Do not run
`npm audit fix --force`: it "fixes" them by downgrading Expo to SDK 44.
`npx expo install --fix` takes Expo 57 patch releases as they ship.

## Native proof

`npm run export:ios` publishes Hermes to `dist/` and returns at every milestone;
native proof then runs in the background at background QoS, so you can keep
building. The next export and `npx snapback4 doctor` print its result; for a
final report run `npx snapback4 witness status --wait`. Receipts and logs stay
in `artifacts/native-smoke/<timestamp>`. `npm run native:smoke` explicitly
waits for a full Release build and smoke. Smoke proves the store opens,
acquisition, cold offline reopen and reconnect; pending write replay needs an
app scenario (`npx snapback4 guide witness`).

## Next steps

- Grow the schema: `npx snapback4 guide language`, then `guide rules`, `guide sync`.
- Try operations from the terminal: `npx snapback4 run add '{"title":"milk"}' --as alice`.
- Test without a server: `npx snapback4 test --fixture fixtures --as alice` loads
  `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and runs the expectations in `fixtures/test.cases.jsonl`
  (`npx snapback4 guide testing`).
- Add email accounts: change `use identity` to `use identity password` and see `guide auth`.
- A complete multi-screen example: `npx snapback4 init <dir> --expo --template chat`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Expanded helpers can write from any expression block.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — legacy eligibility examines the complete expanded write footprint.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn stmts_write_tables(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    for stmt in stmts {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Insert { table, .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "insert")),
            Stmt::Update { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "update")),
            Stmt::Upsert { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "upsert")),
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, upsert, .. } => out.push((target.table.clone(), if *upsert { "upsert" } else { "update" })),
            Stmt::Delete { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "delete")),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => out.push((target.table.clone(), "delete")),
            _ => {}
        }
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr_write_tables(value, out),
            Stmt::Emit { payload: value, once, .. } => { expr_write_tables(value, out); if let Some(value) = once { expr_write_tables(value, out); } },
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                stmts_write_tables(then_body, out);
                stmts_write_tables(else_body, out);
            }
            Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => {
                match domain {
                    LoopDomain::Page(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
                    LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => { expr_write_tables(start, out); expr_write_tables(end_exclusive, out); }
                    LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
                    LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => {
                        expr_write_tables(&recurse.seed, out);
                    }
                    LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } | LoopDomain::ArgList { .. } => {}
                }
                stmts_write_tables(body, out);
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr_write_tables(condition, out),
            Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                for key in &current.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { row, .. } | Stmt::Insert { row, .. } => expr_write_tables(row, out),
            Stmt::Update { key, patch, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
            }
            Stmt::Upsert { key, row, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(row, out);
            }
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, .. } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
                if let Some(condition) = condition { stmts_write_tables(&condition.setup, out); expr_write_tables(&condition.value, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Delete { key, .. } => expr_write_tables(key, out),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue => {}
        }
    }
}

fn scan_write_tables(scan: &Scan, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { expr_write_tables(filter, out); }
    if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
        for bound in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(bound, out); }
    }
    for key in &scan.prefix {
        match key { IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) | IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => expr_write_tables(expr, out) }
    }
    for cursor in [&scan.after, &scan.before].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(cursor, out); }
}

fn expr_write_tables(expr: &Expr, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    match expr {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Variable(_) | Expr::Input(_) => {}
        Expr::Field { base, .. } => expr_write_tables(base, out),
        Expr::Array(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => { expr_write_tables(base, out); fields.values().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)); }
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => {
            expr_write_tables(left, out);
            expr_write_tables(right, out);
        }
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
        Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Get(read) | Expr::Exists(read) => {
            read.key.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out));
        }
        Expr::Block { body, value } => { stmts_write_tables(body, out); expr_write_tables(value, out); }
        Expr::Scan(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            if let Some(after) = merge.position.as_ref().and_then(|position| position.after.as_ref()) { expr_write_tables(after, out); }
            for lane in &merge.lanes {
                if let Some(guard) = &lane.guard { expr_write_tables(guard, out); }
                if let Some(project) = &lane.project { expr_write_tables(project, out); }
                if let Some(value) = &lane.distinct { expr_write_tables(value, out); }
                stmts_write_tables(&lane.setup, out);
                if let Some(source) = &lane.source {
                    if let Some(scan) = source.scan() { scan_write_tables(scan, out); }

pub fn evaluate(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    evaluate_verdict(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|verdict| verdict == Verdict::Readable)
}

pub(super) fn evaluate_verdict(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Verdict, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe);
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok(answer) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|answer| answer.value)
}

pub(super) fn earliest(a: Option<i64>, b: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> {
    match (a, b) { (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)), (a, b) => a.or(b) }
}

struct Timeline {
    value: Verdict,
    changes: Vec<(i64, Verdict)>,
    // Proven from rule structure and verdicts, never from witness deadlines.
    invariant: bool,
}
impl Timeline {
    fn constant(value: Verdict) -> Self { Self { value, changes: Vec::new(), invariant: true } }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let (mut left, mut right) = (self.value, other.value);
        let mut result = Self::constant(left.combine(right, conjunction));
        let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
        result.invariant = (self.invariant && left == decisive)
            || (other.invariant && right == decisive) || (self.invariant && other.invariant);
        let (mut a, mut b) = (self.changes.as_slice(), other.changes.as_slice());
        let mut previous = result.value;
        while let Some(time) = earliest(a.first().map(|(t, _)| *t), b.first().map(|(t, _)| *t)) {
            // All rows expiring at the same instant disappear together. This
            // preserves correlations, including A or not A and A and not A.
            if let Some((head, tail)) = a.split_first() && head.0 == time { left = head.1; a = tail; }
            if let Some((head, tail)) = b.split_first() && head.0 == time { right = head.1; b = tail; }
            let value = left.combine(right, conjunction);
            if value != previous { result.changes.push((time, value)); previous = value; }
        }
        result
    }
}

# TITLE

A Vite + React app over the Snapback 4 backend in `snapback/*.q`, started from
the Snapback 4 web starter.

OFFLINE

```sh
npm install
npx snapback4 check      # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
npx snapback4 dev        # serves http://127.0.0.1:4400 and reloads on save
npm run dev              # in a second terminal: http://localhost:5173
```

SIGN_IN

## Files

- `snapback/*.q`: the backend (tables, rules, `sync`, queries, mutations).
  Edit it and `snapback4 dev` re-adopts it; `snapback4 check` regenerates the API.
- `fixtures/`: your rows as `fixtures/<table>.jsonl` and expectations in
  `fixtures/test.cases.jsonl`, for `npx snapback4 test --fixture fixtures`
  (`npx snapback4 guide testing`).
- `src/client.ts`: one `createApp` per app (or chosen persona). With no synced
  table it uses the browser principal journal; synced tables use `webDevice()`
  for offline reads and predictions. A bare browser `createClient()` refuses
  durable mutations with `E_ADMISSION`.
- `src/App.tsx`: the sign-in and `Home`, a screen to replace. It shows the
  viewer, your operations and your first query without required arguments,
  rendered with `matchRead`. Read with `useQuery(api.op, args)` or
  `usePage(api.list, args)` (a query that takes `c: cursor ?`), write with
  `useMutation(api.op)` (`npx snapback4 guide client`).
- `src/styles.css`, `index.html` (titled after this directory) and
  `vite.config.mts`: yours to change.

## Next steps

- Shapes most apps need (roles, two parties, booking, schedules):
  `npx snapback4 guide recipes`; one screen of forms: `guide cheatsheet`.
- Ship: `VITE_SNAPBACK_URL=https://your-backend npm run build` builds `dist/`
  with an offline app shell (`snapback4 web-shell dist`); serve it over HTTPS.
- A complete multi-screen example: `npx snapback4 init <dir> --web --template chat`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
The read word is renamed to `existence` (revision note above); connection
state keeps its established guide card. The ready arm of `Read<T>` gains a
required discriminant, `existence`, with
three values the SDK computes from facts it already holds:

- **`present`**: the published value is not a top-level `null` or `[]`. A
  `false`, a `0` or `{ liked: false }` is a present answer; only the top
  level counts, never a nested null.
- **`absent`**: the published value is a top-level `null` or `[]`, the
  answer is complete (`complete === true`, `cut === null`,
  no continuation (`null` or omitted)), and this device's pending predicate
  is false for the read: no in-flight device admission, no pending prediction
  with an unknown footprint (which blocks every read), and no pending prediction whose footprint intersects the read's
  tables after the existing complete-local narrowing (`local-query.ts`,
  `LocalReads.pending`, shared by one-shot and observed reads). The
  classification is recomputed whenever the pending set changes, including while `revalidating`, so a retained `absent` never
  survives a prediction that inserts the row.
- **`partial`**: everything else that is ready: `complete === false`, a
  `cut`, a non-null `next`, or an empty value blocked by the pending
  predicate.

`fresh` keeps its meaning and does not demote: an offline `absent` is
absence at the acquired watermark, which the guide says in those words. The
client adds no owner-key or retained-answer proof of its own; the engine
already folds coverage into `complete` (an owner-key partition miss is
`complete`, another principal's key is unknown), and `Coverage` stays
//! The guide is compiled. Every ```quarry block in the language card, the
//! keyword cards and the package README binds with no diagnostic and no
//! note; the language card runs against the engine; every refusal code the
//! system emits is one `why` explains; every guide keyword a diagnostic
//! names is a card; the numbers the cards state are the engine's. A card
//! cannot promise a form the parser refuses (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13).

use snapback4_core::JobState;
use snapback4_core::error::RuntimeError;
use snapback4_core::meter::Ceilings;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
use snapback4_lang::guide::{self, KEYWORDS, LANGUAGE, card, codes, explain_code, language, quarry};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;


#[test]
fn time_waiting_cards_point_to_cli_and_http_clock_controls() {
    for name in ["expire", "jobs", "effects"] {
        let card = snapback4_lang::guide::card(name).unwrap();
        assert!(card.contains("snapback4 clock advance <dur>"), "{name}: missing test pointer");
        assert!(card.contains("POST /dev/clock/advance"), "{name}: missing HTTP pointer");
        assert!(card.contains("snapback4 clock advance 36s\ncurl"), "{name}: missing two-line example");
    }
    assert!(snapback4_lang::guide::card("presence").unwrap().contains("In tests: `snapback4 clock advance 36s`"));
    let readme = README;
    assert!(readme.contains("snapback4 clock advance <dur>"));
    assert!(readme.contains("snapback4 clock advance 36s\n  curl"));
}
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

const README: &str = concat!(include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/SERVER.md"));

// A relocated assertion checks the actual orientation door as well as its destination.
fn readme_reference(name: &str) -> &'static str {
    let map = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_once("## Reference map").unwrap().1;
    assert!(map.contains(&format!("`guide {name}`")), "orientation omits guide {name}");
    card(name).unwrap()
}

#[test]
fn feeds_card_states_background_contract_and_keeps_recipe_refusals() {
    let feeds = card("feeds").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(feeds, readme_reference("feeds"));
    let contract = feeds.split_once("## Following contract").unwrap().1.split_once("## Boundaries").unwrap().0
        .split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    for fact in ["newest first", "Your own events appear immediately", "normally within a second", "copies their newest 50 live events immediately, then the rest of a 1,000-event window", "Older posts remain in `posts` and are not copied.", "Unfollow hides that actor on the next read", "your backfill or cleanup is pending", "Removal hides the event immediately", "without prediction"] {
        assert!(contract.contains(fact), "missing {fact}");
    }
    for bad in ["skips a follower", "skip saturated readers", "A skipped follower", "not skipped\nauthors"] {
        assert!(!feeds.contains(bad), "{bad}");
    }
    for code in ["FEED_PULL_CAP", "FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", "E_RECIPE_RETIRED"] {
        assert_eq!(snapback4_lang::diag::guide_for(code), "feeds");
        assert!(explain_code(code).unwrap().contains("snapback4 guide feeds"));
        assert!(codes().contains(&code));
    }
    for (code, facts) in [
        ("E_RECIPE_RETIRED", vec!["view following: activity", "the engine chooses delivery bounds"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_FILTER", vec!["view activity", "posts where .parentId = null and not .removed"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_READ", vec!["following last 20 by .at after c"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_ACTIVATE", vec!["query home(c: cursor ?): return activity where .actor = viewer or exists follows[viewer, .actor] last 20 by .at after c", "Original event ids are post ids", "--allow-feed-adoption", "5,000,000 copies"]),
        ("FEED_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared authors", "rolled back", "new write ID"]),
        ("FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared-author slots", "rolled back", "public", "pullCounts", "server-run query", "used = 32"]),
    ] {
        let explanation = explain_code(code).unwrap();
        for fact in facts { assert!(explanation.contains(fact), "{code}: missing {fact}"); }
        if ["E_RECIPE_RETIRED", "E_TIMELINE_FILTER", "E_TIMELINE_READ"].contains(&code) {
            for old in ["maintain feed: timeline", "events posts where", "NAME[viewer]"] {
                assert!(!explanation.contains(old), "{code}: why still teaches {old}");
            }
        }
    }
    for retired in ["E_RECIPE_BRIDGE", "E_RECIPE_CAPS"] { assert!(!codes().contains(&retired)); }
}

#[test]
fn w11r1c_residency_fact_cards_require_verbose() {
    for name in ["residency", "sync", "services"] {
        let text = card(name).unwrap();
        assert!(text.contains("check --residency --verbose"), "{name}");
        if name == "sync" { assert!(text.contains("`check --residency` names the proven")); }
        else { assert!(!text.contains("`check --residency`"), "{name}: bare command promises facts"); }
    }
}

#[test]
fn w11r1c_first_screen_generates_types_and_starts_the_backend() {
    let path = README.split_once("### 1. Create and run the backend").unwrap().1.split_once("### 2.").unwrap().0;
    for text in ["npx snapback4 check", "npx snapback4 dev", "npm install"] {
        assert!(path.contains(text), "step 1 omits {text}");
    }
}

# LLP 3000.005: Native jobs and reads — the app's Rust in the data plane, hosted as wasm, metered like a job, the same on the device

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Quarry (`job name(): native`); the engine's job runner and a wasm host (`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/native.rs`); the guest SDK (`crates/snapback4-native`); `check`, `dev`, `run`, `deploy`, `serve`, `doctor`; the guide; the generated Rust types
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1 (for Charlie Cheever)

## Summary

The author asked, after LLP 3000.004: *what if we let you write Rust programs
that could access the database and generate a feed that the normal Snapback
live query could serve — would that be crazy?* Not crazy. It is the open
effects-host question of LLP 2000.000 §10 with a Rust answer, and it fits
Snapback 4 better than the JavaScript options because the pieces exist: the
interpreter runs over a `Rows` trait, the engine already runs jobs as the
system principal one chunk per commit under a meter, and the device core
already compiles to wasm.

This document specifies **native jobs**: a job whose body is not Quarry but
the app's own Rust, compiled to wasm by `snapback4 check`, hosted by the
engine with the same words as any job. Four rules keep it a job rather than a
hole in the design:

1. **Quarry still declares the feed.** The table, its rules, its horizon and
   its `sync` line are in `schema.q`; the Rust fills it. The one-language
   principle holds: effect bodies were always allowed in another language.
2. **Writes go through the engine's write path, never raw SQL**, so rules'
   companions — validation, constraints, maintains, partition events, the
   log — fire, and the ordinary live query serves the result for free.
3. **Reads go through the engine's read path, so the meter counts them.**
   A native job gets a job's words: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings,
   parked on refusal, listed by `doctor`, run by `snapback4 run`.
4. **Pure over the store.** The module imports one host function and
   nothing else: no clock, no randomness, no network. It runs in the
   simulator. Network-bound ranking stays outside and writes into tables
   the native job then reads.

What it buys: floats, vectors, real loops and a typed schema for the scoring
half of a feed, none of which belong in Quarry; and, after the native module
landing, one Rust function over `Rows` that can run on the server as the job
and on the device as the personalizer. What it costs: `check` runs `cargo`
when `snapback/native/` exists (a warm no-op build is a few hundred

## 8. Wave 9: production onboarding and a browser proof

**Implementer:** Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-12 (DX workstream).
The commission covers wave 9 fix list #6, #7, #10, #13, #14 and the jobs
recipe in #18. REL owns the persona-to-password deploy checklist; HAND owns
protocol negotiation and hot-activation envelope consistency.

Password identity produces signup/login forms on web and Expo, retaining the
existing session-store custody. Schema-first init and unmodified-screen adapt
select the password form; edited screens remain application-owned. Both
starters honor runtime `?api=` on web. Expo chooses the existing device-less
journal on web when no table syncs, preserving nativeDevice on native builds.
Expo `build:web` clears Metro on every export so a changed public URL is
compiled; Vite builds again from its current environment.

`check` emits advisory N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER for application tables lacking
an insert/upsert operation or blocked by positive bootstrap dependencies.
Insert rules and read-through parents are evaluated over a least fixed point
of potentially populated tables; jobs write as system, service-seat branches
are potential roots. Negated and unknown comparisons remain potentially
satisfiable: this is a sound conservative warning for the covered positive
fragment, not a general satisfiability proof. Native jobs are opaque and suppress
this absence analysis. A warning points to `guide deploy --section first admin`.
No rule, planner, admission or outbox changes are made. Fixture argument drift
is checked without execution with the existing wire decoder and argument
validator; saved-result references remain unknown. Neither note fails check.

`snapback4 witness web [dist]` serves an existing export on an allocated
loopback port, launches headless Chromium (SNAPBACK4_CHROMIUM overrides cache
and standard-path discovery), opens the real page with `?api=&witness=web`,
then uses its persona or sign-in UI. It calls that app's authenticated SDK
client, using the first generated mutation or `--mutation` and `--args`, and
requires terminal `sent`; failed/pending/timeout are nonzero with a reason.
The app registers its client and generated API with
`registerWebWitness(app, api)` from `snapback4/witness-web`; starters include
this hook. It exists only under the explicit query flag, adds no authority,
and is callable by the page's own scripts already holding that client.
This proves exported client/auth/write integration, not the application's
composer UI or an arbitrary user journey. Missing registration refuses.
A fresh browser and an owned `dev --memory --port 0 --no-watch` make default
runs repeatable without changing the project's durable store. `--url` never
resets a caller-owned server; a deterministic id derived from principal,
operation selection and canonical arguments deduplicates repeated proof
writes under that store's receipt guarantees. Different arguments are new
intents. Credentials are supplied through a named environment variable,
never printed. Cleanup closes the browser/static server and only the owned
backend; long polls never require networkidle.

`upgrade [version]` detects npm/bun/pnpm by packageManager then lockfile,
refusing ambiguous locks. It resolves latest once, preflights core and installed
Expo adapter versions, installs that same exact pin through the manager,
preserves dependency sections and verifies resulting package pins. Native
rebuild and check/acceptance remain explicit. Package-manager failures return
nonzero; its ordinary lockfile/install recovery applies.

The wire table distinguishes raw responses from SDK Read/Write states.
Dropping a null mutation id is a serialization amendment only: a request
without id remains a fresh, non-deduplicated write. Callers mint ids for retries.
No id is fabricated after execution. Omitted ids remain omitted on error paths.

### Wave 9 review dispositions

Round 1 Muse: NOT READY, four material findings. All accepted: wrap the
pre-execution error response in null-id omission; distinguish guest/password
in adaptation state and reporting; retain password README text for Todos/Home
through adaptation; use pnpm's own save-exact/save-dev/save-optional flags.
The first fresh Sol review and the corrected revision's reviews follow under
`llp/reviews/`. Existing card budgets stay unchanged; the photo details now
link to the published client reference beside the new password recipe.

@@ -1602,3 +1602,20 @@ fn guessed_section_words_land_on_one_section_even_in_another_card() {
 
 #[path = "guide/deploy.rs"]
 mod deploy;
+
+#[test]
+fn jobs_catch_up_sweep_includes_rows_before_the_latest_period_and_repeats() {
+    let source = quarry(card("jobs").unwrap()).into_iter().find(|block| block.contains("job releaseDue(")).unwrap();
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("catchup.q".into(), source)]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let mut engine = Engine::new(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([100, 500, 2000].into_iter().enumerate().map(|(n, due)| ("embargoes".into(), Value::Object(BTreeMap::from([
+        ("id".into(), id(&format!("due{n}"))), ("dueAt".into(), Value::Int(due)), ("released".into(), Value::Bool(false)),
+    ]))))).unwrap();
+    for _ in 0..2 {
+        let run = engine.run_job("releaseDue", args(&[("periodStart", Value::Int(400)), ("periodEnd", Value::Int(1000))]), 1000).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(run.state, JobState::Done, "{run:?}");
+        let rows = engine.store().rows("embargoes").unwrap();
+        for row in rows { assert_eq!(row.field("released").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(row.field("dueAt").unwrap().as_int().unwrap() < 1000)); }
+    }
+}

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (042): adapt rewrote the screen but left a README about todos and guest sign-in.
fn readme(dir: &Path, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    let platform = if dir.join("src/App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Web } else if dir.join("App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Expo } else { return };
    let title = super::title(dir);
    let personas = own.identity.is_none() && !own.personas.is_empty();
    let wanted_body = if todos { Body::Todos } else { Body::Home(None) };
    for path in ["README.md", "README.snapback.md"] {
        let Some(text) = read(dir, path) else { continue };
        let port = number_after(&text, "127.0.0.1:");
        let web_port = number_after(&text, "localhost:");
        let ports = [(port, web_port), (None, web_port), (port, None), (None, None)];
        // The offline paragraph names the schema's synced tables when init
        // wrote it; any of them (or 0.4.22's unconditional claim) still matches.
        let masked = starter::without_offline(&text);
        let matched = ports.into_iter().find(|&(port, web_port)| {
            [(Body::Todos, false), (Body::Home(None), false), (Body::Home(None), true)].iter()
                .any(|(body, personas)| [false, true].iter().any(|password| masked == starter::without_offline(&super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, body, *personas, &starter::Offline::Unknown, *password)), port, web_port))))
        });
        let Some((port, web_port)) = matched else { continue };
        let wanted = super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, &wanted_body, personas, &own.offline, own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests))), port, web_port);
        if wanted == text { continue; }
        let about = if todos { "the todo starter's operations, which your snapback/*.q declares again" } else { "your snapback/*.q and the Home screen, named after this directory" };
        let only_offline = !text.starts_with("# Todos") && starter::without_offline(&wanted) == masked;
        // A schema that does not compile right now says nothing about what it syncs.
        if only_offline && own.offline == starter::Offline::Unknown { continue; }
        let why = if only_offline {
            "the unmodified starter README says what works offline for another schema; adapt rewrites that paragraph from the tables your snapback/*.q syncs".to_owned()
        } else {
            format!("the unmodified starter README describes {}; adapt rewrites it to describe {about}", if text.starts_with("# Todos") { "the todo starter (its todos table and guest sign-in)" } else { "the Home screen for another schema" })
        };
        found.push(Leftover { path, why, rewrite: Some(wanted) });
    }
}

#!/usr/bin/env node
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — resolve once, pin core and adapters to that same release.
import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';

function run() {
  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
  if (args.length === 1 && args[0] === '--help') { console.log('usage: snapback4 upgrade [version]\nUpgrade snapback4 and every installed snapback4-expo adapter with npm, bun or pnpm; keep exact pins. Default: latest. Rebuild native apps after changing the adapter.'); return; }
  if (args.length > 1 || (args[0] && !/^(latest|\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?)$/.test(args[0]))) throw Error('E_INPUT: upgrade takes an exact version or latest');
  let root = process.cwd();
  while (!existsSync(join(root, 'package.json'))) { const parent = dirname(root); if (parent === root) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: no project package.json'); root = parent; }
  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
  const declared = pkg.packageManager?.split('@')[0];
  const locks = [['bun', ['bun.lock', 'bun.lockb']], ['pnpm', ['pnpm-lock.yaml']], ['npm', ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json']]].filter(([, files]) => files.some(file => existsSync(join(root, file)))).map(([pm]) => pm);
  if (declared && !['npm', 'bun', 'pnpm'].includes(declared)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: supported package managers are npm, bun and pnpm');
  if (!declared && locks.length > 1) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: multiple package-manager lockfiles; set packageManager in package.json');
  const pm = declared ?? locks[0] ?? 'npm';
  const dependencies = ['dependencies', 'devDependencies', 'optionalDependencies'];
  const targets = ['snapback4', 'snapback4-expo'].filter(name => name === 'snapback4' || dependencies.some(key => pkg[key]?.[name]) || existsSync(join(root, 'node_modules', name, 'package.json')));
  const invoke = (command, capture = false) => {
    const result = spawnSync(pm, command, { cwd: root, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: capture ? ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] : 'inherit' });
    if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${pm} ${command[0]} failed; check registry/tool availability`);
    return result.stdout;
  };
  let version = args[0] ?? 'latest';
  if (version === 'latest') {
    const text = invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'] : ['view', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'], true).trim();
    try { const data = JSON.parse(text); version = typeof data === 'string' ? data : data.version; }
    catch { throw Error('E_UPGRADE: package manager returned no exact core version'); }
  }
  if (typeof version !== 'string' || !/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(version)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: registry returned no exact core version');
  // Check the adapter release before any install changes package.json.
  for (const name of targets) invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'] : ['view', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'], true);
  // Preserve each dependency section. One resolved version, even across sections.
  const groups = new Map();
  for (const name of targets) {
    const sections = dependencies.filter(key => pkg[key]?.[name]);
    if (sections.length > 1) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${name} occurs in multiple dependency sections; consolidate it first`);
    const section = sections[0] ?? 'dependencies';
    if (!groups.has(section)) groups.set(section, []);
    groups.get(section).push(`${name}@${version}`);
  }
  for (const [section, packages] of groups) {
    const mode = section === 'devDependencies' ? (pm === 'bun' ? '--dev' : '--save-dev') : section === 'optionalDependencies' ? (pm === 'bun' ? '--optional' : '--save-optional') : null;
    invoke([pm === 'npm' ? 'install' : 'add', pm === 'bun' ? '--exact' : '--save-exact', ...(mode ? [mode] : []), ...packages]);
  }
  const after = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
  for (const name of targets) if (!dependencies.some(key => after[key]?.[name] === version)) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${name} was not pinned to ${version}`);
  console.log(`upgrade: ${targets.join(', ')} pinned to ${version} with ${pm}; commit the lockfile, run check and acceptance tests${targets.includes('snapback4-expo') ? ', and rebuild the native app' : ''}`);
}
try { run(); } catch (error) { console.error(error.message); process.exitCode = 1; }
fn jobs_catch_up_sweep_includes_rows_before_the_latest_period_and_repeats() {
    let source = quarry(card("jobs").unwrap()).into_iter().find(|block| block.contains("job releaseDue(")).unwrap();
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("catchup.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    let mut engine = Engine::new(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    engine.seed([100, 500, 2000].into_iter().enumerate().map(|(n, due)| ("embargoes".into(), Value::Object(BTreeMap::from([
        ("id".into(), id(&format!("due{n}"))), ("dueAt".into(), Value::Int(due)), ("released".into(), Value::Bool(false)),
    ]))))).unwrap();
    for _ in 0..2 {
        let run = engine.run_job("releaseDue", args(&[("periodStart", Value::Int(400)), ("periodEnd", Value::Int(1000))]), 1000).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(run.state, JobState::Done, "{run:?}");
        let rows = engine.store().rows("embargoes").unwrap();
        for row in rows { assert_eq!(row.field("released").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(row.field("dueAt").unwrap().as_int().unwrap() < 1000)); }
    }
}

#[test]
fn unavailable_http_body_is_incomplete_for_legacy_clients_and_hides_release_meters() {
    for dev in [true, false] {
        let host = Host::with_store(dev, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),
            "use identity\nquery costly():\n  text = ''\n  for n in range(0, 10000):\n    text = text + 'an allocation that stays bounded by the heap meter'\n  return [text]\n");
        let session = body(&host.request("POST", "/auth/guest", "", "{}"));
        let headers = format!("Authorization: Bearer {}\r\n", session["session"]["token"].as_str().unwrap());
        let response = host.request("POST", "/q/costly", &headers, r#"{"args":{}}"#);
        assert_eq!(status(&response), 200, "{response}");
        let reply = body(&response);
        assert_eq!(reply["loading"], true);
        assert_eq!(reply["store_id"], lock(&host.shared.engine).store().meta("store_id").unwrap().unwrap());
        assert!(reply.get("data").is_some_and(Json::is_null), "legacy data placeholder");
        assert_eq!(reply["complete"], false, "legacy clients must not cache a complete undefined answer");
        assert_eq!(reply.get("meter").is_some(), dev, "production omits the field entirely");
    }
}

pub(super) struct Host {
    server: Arc<Server>,
    pub(super) shared: Arc<Shared>,
    admission: Arc<RequestAdmission>,
    worker: Option<JoinHandle<()>>,
    address: SocketAddr,
}
impl Host {
    fn new(dev: bool) -> Self {
        Self::with_store(dev, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), SCHEMA)
    }

    pub(super) fn with_store(dev: bool, store: snapback4_core::Store, source: &str) -> Self {
        Self::with_store_on(dev, store, source, "127.0.0.1")
    }
    fn with_store_on(dev: bool, store: snapback4_core::Store, source: &str, bind: &str) -> Self {
        Self::with_evidence(dev, store, source, bind, None)
    }
    fn request(&self, method: &str, path: &str, headers: &str, body: &str) -> String {
        exchange(
            self.address,
            &format!(
                "{method} {path} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n{headers}\r\n{body}",
                body.len()
            ),
        )
    }
}

A failure-path test found a password leak: Playwright includes the value passed to `fill()` in its timeout error, and the witness prints that error to stderr. I reproduced it with a fake password and a disabled password field. That violates section 8’s “never printed” requirement. The README adaptation code has since changed, so I’ll recheck it before including that finding.

## Upgrade

`bun add snapback4@latest` upgrades the core package and its platform CLI.
For Expo, use **one command**: `bun add snapback4@latest snapback4-expo@latest`.
The adapter requires the exact core version, including the patch; `check`
warns on stderr if installed versions differ (also with --json). Rebuild your native development/release app
after changing the adapter (Expo Go uses the online tier). Commit the lockfile,
run `bunx snapback4 check`, typecheck the app and run your acceptance tests.
`snapback4 test` uses ./fixtures when present; `--fixture dir` overrides it.

## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}`; never treat it as an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

fn screens(dir: &Path, platform: Platform, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>, advice: &mut Vec<String>) {
    let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
    let (Some(app), Some(client)) = (read(dir, app_path), read(dir, client_path)) else { return };
    // Which starter screen is on disk, if both files are still as init wrote
    // them: its sign-in (guest, or a persona picker) and its body.
    let journal_shape = starter::Offline::Tables { synced: vec![], total: 0 };
    let client_shape = normalized(&client);
    let matches_client = |reference: &str| client_shape == normalized(reference)
        || client_shape == normalized(&starter::web_client(reference.to_owned(), &journal_shape));
    let current = [Body::Todos, Body::Home(first_read(&app))].into_iter().find_map(|body| {
        let (guest_app, guest_client) = starter::screens(platform, None, &body);
        let (persona_app, persona_client) = starter::screens(platform, Some(&[]), &body);
        let (password_app, password_client) = starter::identity_screens(platform, None, &body, true);
        if app == guest_app && matches_client(&guest_client) { Some((SignIn::Guest, body)) }
        else if app == password_app && matches_client(&password_client) { Some((SignIn::Password, body)) }
        else if app == persona_app && matches_client(&persona_client) { Some((SignIn::Personas(personas_of(&client)), body)) }
        else { None }
    });
    let Some((sign_in, body)) = current else { return };
    // A Home keeps its read while the schema still declares that query
    // argument-free; a Home without one stays as written (it fits any schema).
    let wanted_body = match &body {
        _ if todos => Body::Todos,
        Body::Home(None) => Body::Home(None),
        Body::Home(Some(op)) if own.reads.contains(op) => body.clone(),
        _ => Body::Home(own.reads.first().cloned()),
    };
    // A sign-in no starter screen fits keeps the one on disk, with advice.

+#[test]
+fn password_starters_and_expo_web_journal_follow_schema() {
+    for flag in ["--web", "--expo"] {
+        for identity in ["use identity password", "use personas alice"] {
+            let dir = Dir::new(); fs::create_dir(dir.0.join("snapback")).unwrap();
+            fs::write(dir.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), format!("{identity}\ntable notes:\n  body: text 1..80\n  public\n  online only\nmutation add(body: text 1..80):\n  return insert notes {{ body }}\n")).unwrap();
+            let out = dir.run(flag); assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
+            let app = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join(if flag == "--expo" { "App.tsx" } else { "src/App.tsx" })).unwrap();
+            if identity.contains("password") { assert!(app.contains("auth.signup") && app.contains("auth.login") && !app.contains("auth.guest"), "{app}"); }
+            let client = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("src/client.ts")).unwrap();
+            assert!(!client.contains("webDevice"), "{client}");
+            assert!(client.contains("get(\"api\")") && client.contains("registerWebWitness"), "{client}");
+            if flag == "--expo" { assert!(client.contains("nativeDevice"), "{client}"); }
+            let pkg: Value = serde_json::from_str(&fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("package.json")).unwrap()).unwrap();
+            assert!(pkg["scripts"]["build:web"].is_string());
+            if flag == "--expo" { assert!(pkg["scripts"]["build:web"].as_str().unwrap().contains("--clear")); }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn adapt_switches_guest_password_and_persona_and_keeps_password_readme() {
+    for mode in ["--web", "--expo"] {
+        let dir = Dir::new(); assert!(dir.run(mode).status.success());
+        let path = dir.0.join("snapback/schema.q");
+        let original = fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
+        for (identity, expected) in [("use identity password", "the password sign-in"), ("use personas alice", "a persona picker"), ("use identity password", "the password sign-in"), ("use identity", "the guest sign-in")] {
+            let body = original.lines().filter(|line| !line.starts_with("use identity") && !line.starts_with("use personas")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
+            fs::write(&path, format!("{identity}\n{body}\n")).unwrap();
+            let out = dir.init(&["--adapt"]); assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
+            let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout); assert!(text.contains(expected), "{text}");
+            let app = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join(if mode == "--web" { "src/App.tsx" } else { "App.tsx" })).unwrap();
+            if identity.contains("password") {
+                assert!(app.contains("auth.signup") && !app.contains("auth.guest"));
+                let readme = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap();
+                assert!(!readme.contains("Choose \"Continue as a"), "{readme}");
+                assert!(readme.contains("Sign up or sign in"), "{readme}");
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

codex
The remaining findings are the password leak, the owned-child leak during startup, and the envelope table’s omission of raw `{loading:true, data:null, complete:false}` replies. I reproduced the latter with a meter-limited query. Password adaptation now updates both the form and README. The new catch-up test still fails because `catchup.q` namespaces its job while the fixture uses a bare schedule target; the recipe should be checked as `schema.q`.

async function backend(timeout, own) {
  const child = spawn(process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN, ['dev', '--memory', '--port', '0', '--no-watch'], { stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'], env: { ...process.env, SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID: String(process.pid) } });
  let output = '', errors = '';
  child.stderr.on('data', bytes => { errors = (errors + bytes).slice(-4096); });
  const close = async () => {
    if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) return;
    const done = new Promise(yes => child.once('close', yes)); child.kill('SIGTERM');
    const timer = setTimeout(() => child.kill('SIGKILL'), 5000); await done; clearTimeout(timer);
  };
  own({ close }); // Record ownership immediately, including readiness and signal races.
  try {
    const url = await new Promise((yes, no) => {
      const timer = setTimeout(() => no(Error('E_WITNESS_BACKEND: dev did not become ready')), timeout);
      child.once('error', () => { clearTimeout(timer); no(Error('E_WITNESS_BACKEND: cannot spawn snapback4 dev')); });
      child.once('exit', () => { clearTimeout(timer); no(Error(`E_WITNESS_BACKEND: dev exited before readiness; ${errors}`)); });
      child.stdout.on('data', bytes => { output += bytes; const line = output.split('\n')[0]; if (output.includes('\n') && /^http:\/\/127\.0\.0\.1:\d+$/.test(line)) { clearTimeout(timer); yes(line); } });
    });
    return { url, close };
  } catch (error) { await close(); throw error; }
}
const canonical = value => Array.isArray(value) ? value.map(canonical) : value && typeof value === 'object' ? Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(value).sort().map(key => [key, canonical(value[key])])) : value;
async function run(o) {
  const browserPath = executable();
  let dev, web, browser;
  let cleanupPromise;
  const cleanup = () => cleanupPromise ??= Promise.allSettled([browser?.close(), web?.close(), dev?.close()]);
  const interrupted = () => { void cleanup().finally(() => process.exit(130)); };
  process.once('SIGINT', interrupted); process.once('SIGTERM', interrupted);
  try {
    web = await webServer(o.export);
    dev = o.url ? null : await backend(o.timeout * 1000, owned => { dev = owned; });
    const api = o.url ?? dev.url;
    browser = await chromium.launch({ executablePath: browserPath, headless: true, timeout: o.timeout * 1000, args: process.getuid?.() === 0 ? ['--no-sandbox'] : [] });
    const page = await browser.newPage(); // Fresh storage; the external backend is never reset.
    page.setDefaultTimeout(o.timeout * 1000);
    let pageError;
    page.on('pageerror', error => { pageError = error.message; });
    const url = new URL(web.url); url.searchParams.set('api', api); url.searchParams.set('witness', 'web');
    if (o.as) url.searchParams.set('as', o.as);
    await page.goto(url.href, { waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' });
    if (o.email) {
    for name in ["testing", "rules", "recipes", "reads", "client", "wire"] { assert_eq!(guide::piped(name, card(name).unwrap()), card(name).unwrap(), "{name} prints whole"); }
    assert!(guide::piped("language", card("language").unwrap()).lines().count() < 150, "the language card stays an overview");
    assert!(whole * 4 >= KEYWORDS.len() * 3, "most cards print whole: {whole}/{}", KEYWORDS.len());
}

// Wave 7: `--section` words builders guessed (008 009 023 065 070 092).
#[test]
fn guessed_section_words_land_on_one_section_even_in_another_card() {
    let land = |name: &str, words: &str| guide::section(name, card(name).unwrap(), words).unwrap_or_else(|miss| panic!("guide {name} --section {words}: {miss}"));
    let wire = land("wire", "acceptance");
    assert!(wire.starts_with("No heading of guide wire is named \"acceptance\"; it is a section of guide testing") && wire.contains("## An acceptance script over HTTP"), "{wire}");
    for (name, words, heading) in [("language", "statements", "## Details"), ("writes", "statements", "## Details"), ("writes", "loops", "### Loops"), ("language", "loops", "### Loops"),
        ("client", "react", "## React"), ("client", "hooks", "## React"), ("client", "React", "## React")] {
        let text = land(name, words);
        assert!(text.lines().any(|line| line.starts_with(heading)), "{name} --section {words}: {text}");
        assert!(text.lines().count() < card(if name == "language" && words == "statements" { "writes" } else if name == "writes" && words == "loops" { "language" } else { name }).unwrap().lines().count());
    }
    // A plurality of mentions lands on one section and names the others; a tie lists the mentions.
    let count = land("totals", "count");
    assert!(count.starts_with("No heading of guide totals is named \"count\"; 0. Opening holds") && count.contains("; also "), "{count}");
    assert!(card("react") == card("client") && guide::alias_landing("react", "client", card("client").unwrap()).unwrap().contains("matchRead"));
    assert_eq!(card("money"), card("types"));
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn jobs_catch_up_sweep_includes_rows_before_the_latest_period_and_repeats() {
    let source = quarry(card("jobs").unwrap()).into_iter().find(|block| block.contains("job releaseDue(")).unwrap();
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    let mut engine = Engine::new(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    engine.seed([100, 500, 2000].into_iter().enumerate().map(|(n, due)| ("embargoes".into(), Value::Row(BTreeMap::from([
        ("id".into(), id(&format!("due{n}"))), ("dueAt".into(), Value::Int(due)), ("released".into(), Value::Bool(false)),
    ]).into())))).unwrap();
    for _ in 0..2 {
        let run = engine.run_job("releaseDue", args(&[("periodStart", Value::Int(400)), ("periodEnd", Value::Int(1000))]), 1000).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(run.state, JobState::Done, "{run:?}");
        let rows = engine.store().rows("embargoes").unwrap();
        for row in rows { assert_eq!(row.field("released").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(row.field("dueAt").unwrap().as_int().unwrap() < 1000)); }
    }
}
## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}`; never treat it as an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

NOT READY

Two MATERIAL findings remain in the latest inspected files:

1. **Password credentials can reach stderr.** [witness-web.js](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w9-dx/snapback4/packages/snapback4/bin/witness-web.js:107) passes the password to Playwright’s `fill()`, then prints exception messages verbatim. With a disabled password input, I reproduced a timeout whose call log contained `fill("fake-proof-secret-917")`. This violates section 8’s “never printed” requirement. **Repair:** replace credential-entry exceptions with a fixed auth diagnostic and sanitize subsequent diagnostics for the supplied password. Add a failure-path test asserting the password appears in neither stdout nor stderr.

2. **The new envelope table omits raw HTTP loading replies.** [wire.md](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w9-dx/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md:31) lists query answers and denials, but [serve.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w9-dx/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:1238) also returns HTTP 200 with `{loading:true, data:null, complete:false, …}`. I reproduced this with a meter-limited query. Its null is a placeholder, not an answer. **Repair:** document this third raw-query envelope and explicitly require checking `loading` before interpreting `data`. Also qualify [client.md’s](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w9-dx/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/client.md:92) “empty complete list is absent”: LLP 3000.031 §1.1 additionally requires no cut, no continuation, and no blocking pending predicate.

Minor suggestions:

- Replace [deploy.md’s upgrade instructions](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w9-dx/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:15) with `snapback4 upgrade`; they still recommend independently resolving `@latest`.
- Add writer-lint regressions for multi-table positive cycles, read-through parents, system-job roots, and opaque native jobs. Current coverage is much narrower than the amendment’s stated fragment.

Verification: all **8 current wave9 JS tests passed**, including exported web writes and Expo client typechecks; upgrade tests mock the managers. Focused Rust writer, fixture, starter, and adaptation tests passed. Manual HTTP probes confirmed omitted IDs on sent/refused replies. The startup child leak was repaired during review, and my repeated SIGTERM probe confirmed cleanup.

I found no additional MATERIAL defect in the conservative lints, current password adaptation, deterministic witness IDs, corrected package-manager flags, or narrow null-ID serialization. No files were edited.
